--- Page 5 ---
# Purpose of Beginning the Book

O Lord, the books collected by you that are circulating on this earth certainly shine for the delight of the wise. Having studied them, I, your wife, beg you to compose a new work on **Women’s Horoscopy** <note>original: "Stri-Jatakam"; a branch of astrology focusing specifically on the lives and destinies of women</note> which is capable of benefiting humanity. ॥ 1 ॥

By which the understanding of a woman’s good or ill fortune may be properly attained, being most skillful in the act of helping them. Having heard these words spoken by his beloved, **Shri Shyamlal** <note>The author of the text</note>, his mind being stirred for that purpose, ॥ 2 ॥

Spoke to his dear, loving wife with affection: "I am truly won over by you because of your effort toward the welfare of others. Since you are so inclined, remember that I am now calling upon the praiseworthy God to fulfill this desired goal." ॥ 3 ॥

"May that God be fully sufficient to grant your wish, O dear one—He who, in order to protect the group of cowherds from the flood of rain, crushed the pride of Indra <note>original: "Jaishnavam"; referring to the King of the Gods</note> and playfully lifted the great mountain **Govardhan** <note>A sacred hill in Braj, India</note>, holding it upon his little finger <term>kanishthika</term> <note>the smallest finger of the hand</note> for seven days." ॥ 4 ॥

<vocab>Purpose of beginning the book, Women's Horoscopy, Shyamlal, Govardhan, Little finger, Praiseworthy</vocab>

<summary>This opening page establishes the motivation for the text: the author, Shyamlal, is requested by his wife to write a treatise on women's astrology for the benefit of the world, which he begins with an invocation to Lord Krishna.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, women's horoscopy, Shyamlal, Govardhan, Krishna, Indra, Sanskrit literature</keywords>

--- Page 6 ---
# Introduction
## An Announcement to the Lovers of the Delight of Astrology

Having meditated upon the lotus feet of Lord Krishna—the source of bliss, the son of Nanda, the benefactor of his devotees, the destroyer of demons, the humbler of Indra's pride, and the lifter of Mount Govardhan—I first offer my salutations with folded hands to the lotus feet of the most compassionate scholars residing in the land of the Aryans <note>original: "Aryavarta," a historical name for Northern India</note>. Now, one should observe what various wonderful sciences the Supreme Brahman, the Almighty, has created for the benefit of the world in this fleeting existence. By knowing these sciences, the human body—composed of the five elements <note>earth, water, fire, air, and ether</note>—is considered the foremost among the eighty-four million species created by Lord Brahma. Often, those who master these sciences become as worshipful as the gods among men, and kings and emperors grant them great honor. 

At this time, there is no need to describe other sciences. I submit only regarding the science of Astrology <term>Jyotish: the traditional Indian system of astronomy and astrology</term>, which is the root of all religions that benefit the world. By knowing this Science of Hours <term>Horashastra: predictive astrology based on the time of birth</term>, those who see through the three periods of time <note>past, present, and future</note> can explain the auspicious and inauspicious fruits of all three births for every living being. Those virtuous men who follow the rules of this science never experience any sorrow. Within this field, many branches are described, such as Mathematical Astronomy <term>Siddhanta</term>, Omens and Natural Astrology <term>Samhita</term>, Natal Astrology <term>Hora Jataka</term>, Annual Persian-influenced Astrology <term>Tajika</term>, Horary Astrology <term>Prashna</term>, and Electional Astrology <term>Muhurta</term>. Among these, all worldly people consider the Natal branch <term>Jataka</term> to be the best, because through it, the appropriate results of a person's past, future, and present are told. It has two divisions: one is the Male Horoscope, and the other is the Female Horoscope. Thus—

<gloss>Introduction, Astrology, Science of Hours, Natal Astrology, Mathematical Astronomy, Compendium/Omens, Annual Astrology, Inquiry/Horary, Auspicious Timing, Five Elements, Land of the Aryans, Science of Light/Astrology, Seer of Three Times</gloss>

<summary>This introductory page serves as a formal opening and invocation for an astrological treatise, establishing Astrology (Jyotish) as a divine science essential for understanding human destiny across past, present, and future lives.</summary>

<header>Introduction</header>

# Introduction

Previously, for the benefit of interpreting the birth charts of men, I authored the work titled *Jyotish-Shyam-Sangraha* <note>A Compendium of Astrology by Shyam</note> in the year 1896 <note>original: Samvat 1953</note>. This was published through the grace of Seth Gangavishnu Shrikrishnadas at the "Lakshmi Venkateshwar" Press in Bombay. Now, for the benefit of interpreting the fortunes of women, and with the assistance of the Great King of Kings, the Crest-Jewel of Heroes, the Pillar of Dharma, the Ruler of the Kahlur State and Lord of Bilaspur, His Excellence 108 Maharaja Vijay Chand Ji Dev, I am publishing this second work titled *Strijataka* <note>Horoscopy of Women</note>. 

This work has been compiled from the treatises composed by ancient masters such as Vashistha, Vriddhayavana, Shaunaka, Garga, Pitamaha, Narapati, Shripati, Skanda, Varahamihira, Gunakara, Balabhadrasuri, Dhundhiraj, Bhrigu, Bharadwaj, Devakirti, Narada, Ganesh Daivajna, Ram Daivajna, and the contemporary Shyam Daivajna. I am publishing it along with a Hindi commentary named "Shyamsundari." 

In this world, woman is the primary cause of physical happiness for man; indeed, the ancient masters have called woman the "Achiever of the Three Goals of Life" <note>original: Trivargasadhini; referring to Virtue (Dharma), Wealth (Artha), and Pleasure (Kama)</note>. Those men in whose homes reside virtuous women live free from anxiety, enjoying all worldly comforts and attaining fame throughout their lives. Conversely, those men in whose homes reside ill-mannered or quarrelsome women suffer various miseries day and night throughout their lives and remain recipients of infamy in the world. This is as the ancient masters have stated:

->**Verse:** "By their auspicious marks and virtuous conduct, women—who are the very abode of joy—can grant long life even to a husband destined for a short life. (1) Therefore, wise men should marry women possessing good qualities, having first examined their characteristics and avoided those with ill-omened traits." (2)<-

This means that a woman, through her good physical signs and noble character, can extend the lifespan of a husband who has a short life expectancy. <insert>Conversely, one with evil signs and bad character can shorten the life of a long-lived man.</insert> (1) For this reason...

<summary>The author introduces his new work on female horoscopy, compiled from classical astrological authorities under the patronage of Maharaja Vijay Chand of Bilaspur. He emphasizes the profound influence a wife's character and astrological signs have on a husband's longevity and worldly success.</summary>

<keywords>Strijataka, Astrology, Bilaspur State, Maharaja Vijay Chand, Women's Horoscopes, Trivarga, Domestic Happiness, Auspicious Signs</keywords>

--- Page 8 ---
<meta>This page continues the introductory preface regarding the selection of a bride and the astrological considerations necessary for a successful marriage.</meta>

<page-num>4</page-num>
-># Introduction<-

A clever man should marry a woman who possesses auspicious physical and spiritual signs. One must first examine these signs and reject a maiden who bears inauspicious traits. 2 For this reason, as far as possible before marriage, one should consult a wise scholar using this <term>Balavani Jataka</term><note>A specific astrological treatise or system for analyzing birth charts, likely the title of this manuscript</note> to evaluate a girl from a noble family of one's own <term>Varna</term><note>Social class or caste</note>. Through her <term>Janmapatra</term><note>Birth chart or horoscope</note>, one should consider her appearance, character, virtues, fortune, potential for children, and her faithfulness. Those men who, before marriage, investigate these matters through this book—or, if any flaws are discovered in the girl's horoscope, perform the pacification rituals at the time of the wedding as prescribed in this text—will never encounter sorrows related to their wives, even in their dreams. Throughout their lives in this world, they will enjoy every happiness from their wife's side and will attain the four goals of life: <term>Dharma</term><note>Duty/Righteousness</note>, <term>Artha</term><note>Prosperity</note>, <term>Kama</term><note>Pleasure</note>, and <term>Moksha</term><note>Liberation</note>.

Now, the father of the maiden should abandon the greed for money. Just as the <term>Kuyoga</term><note>Inauspicious planetary alignments</note> are described for the bride's side, he should likewise have the groom's horoscope thoroughly examined using male-oriented astrological texts. After observing the groom's virtues and flaws and determining his lifespan and other factors, he should marry the girl to a healthy, handsome, and noble groom who is approximately one and a half times the age of the bride. By acting contrary to this, the girl's mother, father, elder brother, or whoever holds the right of <term>Kanyadaan</term><note>The ritual act of giving away the bride</note> becomes a participant in the sins of hell.

Nowadays, the <term>Purohits</term><note>Family priests</note> and <term>Padhas</term><note>Village teachers or ritualists</note> have established a system where they only haphazardly check eight specific factors in the horoscopes of the bride and groom: the class, dominance, star, temperament, planetary friendship, clan, friendship of categories, and the pulse <note>original: "Varna, Vashya, Tara, Yoni, Graha-maitri, Gana-maitri, Bhakoot, Nadi"</note>. Having looked at these eight things in a superficial manner, they proceed fearlessly...

<column-break/>

# Page 5
## Introduction

The father of the bride or groom is told that the horoscopes <note>Sanskrit: *kundali*; a birth chart used to determine destiny and compatibility</note> match perfectly. However, those specific factors which ought to be examined in the couple's horoscopes are completely ignored by the local priests <note>original: *padha*; often used here to refer to village priests who may lack deep scholarly training</note>. 

The father of the bride should have the groom's horoscope examined by a truly learned scholar for negative indications such as:
*   **Lifespan**
*   **Leprosy** and similar ailments
*   **Royal diseases** <note>original: *rajaroga*; typically referring to tuberculosis or other chronic, wasting diseases</note>
*   **Renunciation** <note>original: *pravrajya*; an astrological combination suggesting the person will abandon worldly life to become a monk</note>
*   **Excommunication from kin**
*   **Outcast status** <note>original: *mleccha*; referring to those outside the traditional social or moral order</note>
*   And other various misfortunes.

Similarly, the father of the groom should examine the bride's horoscope for signs of **widowhood**, **childlessness**, **wicked character**, **insanity**, **adultery**, **chronic illness**, and other such ill-fated combinations before finalizing the union. If this is not done, the couple must suffer for a lifetime, and the sin of that suffering will fall upon those priests who failed to properly match the horoscopes before the wedding. 

However, the horoscopes of the bride and groom must be calculated based on the correct "Ishta" <note>the specific time elapsed since sunrise, used to calculate the ascendant</note>. Inaccurate birth records are the very root of the mutual misery experienced by husbands and wives. When the time for a child's birth approaches, one should summon a skilled mathematician <note>original: *ganitagna*; a specialist in astronomical and astrological calculations</note> to be present beforehand, so that the exact moment of the rising sign <note>original: *lagna*</note> is not missed or corrupted. 

Before marriage, one must have a truly wise scholar perform the "Melana" <note>the formal process of matching eight specific categories of compatibility</note> and the purification of the wedding time, so that this mental and ancestral suffering does not have to be endured for a lifetime. 

Alas! Such darkness prevails in the world today. Whether the horoscope is accurate or not, whether the bride suffers or the groom, the patrons <note>original: *yajaman*; the person who commissions a religious ritual</note> simply call upon those ignorant "Star-Gazers" <note>original: *nakshatrasuchi*; a derogatory term for priests who pretend to know astrology but lack true depth</note> to settle everything. 

Grief! Grief! Grief!! Grief!!! Regarding the birth of a child or the marriage of a son or daughter, even the poorest of men will wastefully spend hundreds of rupees on prostitutes, performers, and fireworks. Yet, no one thinks to give ten or twenty rupees to an intelligent astrologer to ensure an accurate birth record or a proper matching of horoscopes for the wedding.

<vocab>Introduction, Bride and Groom, Horoscope, Village Priest, Chronic Disease, Renunciation, Loss of Caste, Outcast, Widowhood, Childlessness, Adultery, Mathematician, Purification/Correction, Pseudo-Astrologer, Patron</vocab>

<summary>The author critiques the negligence of parents and unlearned priests who fail to properly analyze astrological compatibility, leading to lifelong marital misery. He urges the hiring of skilled mathematicians to record birth times accurately and warns against wasting money on celebrations while neglecting vital astrological safeguards.</summary>

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>This page continues the introductory remarks regarding the necessity of specialized astrological texts for women, establishing the rationale for the author's new work.</meta>

# Introduction

One should ensure the purification of the <term>Lagna</term> <note>The Ascendant or the specific point of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth</note>. For this very reason, the children of those men who neglect this must endure many hardships throughout their lives. Nowadays, it is frequently observed that learned priests tend to declare the results of women's birth charts using texts intended for men, such as the *Purusha Jataka*. This is entirely incorrect. Scholars ought to declare all auspicious and inauspicious results of a man's birth chart from <term>Purusha Jataka</term> <note>Astrological treatises specifically for male subjects</note> and <term>Brihat Jataka</term> <note>A foundational 6th-century Sanskrit text on Hindu astrology</note>, while the results for women's birth charts should be considered from <term>Stri Jataka</term> <note>Astrological treatises specifically for female subjects</note> texts. It is only appropriate to calculate the determination of lifespan and the <term>Dasha</term> <note>Planetary periods that govern specific times in a person's life</note> in the same manner for both. Furthermore, any <term>Rajayoga</term> <note>Planetary combinations indicating royalty, high status, or great fortune</note> or fortune-bestowing combinations found in a woman's birth chart must be attributed to her husband. 

Now, when scholars search for texts on women's astrology, they find no more than twenty-five or thirty verses anywhere. Therefore, I have compiled a new, extensive volume titled **Balvarni Jataka**, gathering the consensus of many ancient teachers for the benefit of interpreting women's fortunes. Those scholars who consider the complete auspicious and inauspicious results of a woman's birth chart and speak of the past, future, and present through this book will find their predictions to be accurate according to the times. Moreover, those residents of India who have a girl's birth chart examined before marriage and observe all her characteristics through this book will, by the grace of God, never experience even a dream of suffering from the wife's side. They will likely obtain all happiness, including sons and grandsons. I hope that the most merciful and excellent scholars will forgive my audacity in this undertaking. Instead, I hope they will bless this humble servant and make my labor fruitful. And should there be any error in this book, then...

<summary>The author argues that women's horoscopes must be interpreted using specialized female-centric texts rather than male ones and introduces his comprehensive new work, the Balvarni Jataka.</summary>
<keywords>Lagna, birth chart, Stri Jataka, Balvarni Jataka, marriage, Rajayoga, female astrology, Indian astrology</keywords>

<meta>This page serves as the table of contents and structural overview for the treatise, following the introductory invocations of the previous pages.</meta>

# Introduction

Abandoning pride, one should purify themselves. This Hindi commentary on the text, titled **"Shyamsundari"** <note>Literally "The Dark Beauty," likely named in honor of a deity or as a poetic title for the commentary</note>, has been composed in simple language and the work is adorned with eighteen chapters.

**Chapter One:** In which the general characteristics of a maiden are described.
**Chapter Two:** In this, the characteristics of the sixty-six (66) limbs from the toenails to the top of the head <note>The traditional *Nakh-Shikh* description style</note> and all their auspicious and inauspicious results are described in detail.
**Chapter Three:** In which the characteristics and results of marks such as moles and spots <term>Til-Mashaka: moles and lentigines used in physiognomy</term> on all parts of a maiden's body are stated.
**Chapter Four:** In which the consideration of the *Trishansha* <term>a 1/30th division of a zodiac sign used in astrology to determine character</term> from the maiden's birth chart is described.
**Chapter Five:** In which the results of various planetary combinations and the influence of the Sun and other planets based on the *Navamsha* <term>a 1/9th divisional chart</term> located in the seventh house are explained, along with many combinations for the cause of a maiden's death, her renunciation <term>Pravrajya: taking up a monastic or ascetic life</term>, and marital happiness.
**Chapter Six:** In which the *Rajayogas* <note>Planetary combinations indicating royalty, wealth, or great success</note> for women are illustrated with examples of birth charts and diagrams.
**Chapter Seven:** In which the results of the lunar dates <term>Tithis</term> of a maiden's birth, from the first day of the lunar fortnight to the full moon, are stated.
**Chapter Eight:** In this, the results of the seven days of the week, from Sunday onwards, at the time of a maiden's birth are stated.
**Chapter Nine:** In this, the results of being born under the twenty-seven (27) constellations <term>Nakshatras</term>, from *Ashwini* to *Revati*, are stated.
**Chapter Ten:** In this, the results of the *Vishkumbha* and other astrological turnings <term>Yogas</term> at the time of a girl's birth are described.
**Chapter Eleven:** In this, the results of being born during the *Bava* and other astrological periods <term>Karanas</term> are stated.
**Chapter Twelve:** In this, the results of birth according to the rising signs <term>Lagnas</term>, from Aries onwards, are stated.
**Chapter Thirteen:** In this, the results of birth according to the Moon sign <term>Rashis</term> are stated.
**Chapter Fourteen:** In this, the results of the Sun and other planets, including *Rahu* <note>The North Node of the Moon</note>, as they are situated in the various houses of the birth chart are stated.
**Chapter Fifteen:** In this, the consideration of being born under the "un-enjoyed" root constellation <term>Abhukta-Mula: a particularly inauspicious astrological timing</term> and its specific rituals are described.
**Chapter Sixteen:** In this, the peace-making rituals <term>Shanti: rites to neutralize negative planetary influences</term> for the aforementioned *Mula* birth are provided.
**Chapter Seventeen:** In this, the *Ashlesha Mula* <note>A specific inauspicious astrological combination</note>...

<summary>This page outlines the first seventeen chapters of the "Shyamsundari" commentary, a comprehensive guide to feminine astrology and physiognomy. It details how the text covers physical traits, birth charts, and ritual remedies for inauspicious planetary alignments.</summary>

<meta>This page concludes the introductory section (Preface) of the manuscript, detailing the final chapters and the author's closing appeal to the reader.</meta>

<page-num>8</page-num>
# Introduction

The descriptions of the three types of *Gandanta* <note>critical transitional points between lunar mansions or zodiac signs</note>, the *Ganda* defects, the classifications of the *Nakshatras* <note>lunar mansions</note>, charitable giving, the pacification rituals for *Jyeshtha*, *Revati*, and *Ganda*, the results of being born under the quarters of *Jyeshtha*, and the pacification of harmful astrological combinations—namely *Vyatipata*, *Vaidhriti*, and the *Sankranti* <note>the sun's transition into a new sign</note>—have been provided. Furthermore, the pacification rituals for births occurring during *Kuhu* <note>new moon</note>, *Sinivali* <note>the day when the moon is first visible</note>, the *Darsha* <note>conjunction of sun and moon</note> rituals, the pacification for births on the fourteenth day of the dark fortnight, the rituals for siblings born under the same star, the pacification of birth abnormalities, and the rituals for births during solar and lunar eclipses have been described. By performing these pacification rituals, the ill effects surrounding a girl are removed and auspicious results are obtained. **Chapter Eighteen:** In this chapter, the lineage of the author is described. **Prayer:** If any errors have occurred in this book due to a slip of the hand or printing mistakes, may the eminent scholars correct them and always look with favor upon me, their humble servant. And if any virtuous person has any question regarding this book, they may gratify me by sending it via letter to the address written below.

->One who desires the grace of the Brahmins—Royal Astrologer Pandit Shyamlal Sharma,<-
->Residence—Bans-Bareilly<-

<summary>This page outlines the final chapters of the text concerning astrological pacification rituals (Shanti) for various inauspicious birth conditions and concludes with the author's humble request for corrections and correspondence.</summary>

### Auspiciousness

# Now Begins the Stree Jataka
## With the Shyam Sundari Hindi Commentary

Having joyfully bowed to my parents and to the Supporter of Mount Govardhan <note>A reference to Lord Krishna, who lifted the Govardhan hill to protect his devotees</note>, I, Shyam Lall, being devoted to women, compose this work titled *Stree Jataka* <note>original: "Stree Jataka"; a treatise on female horoscopy and characteristics</note>. ||1||

**Meaning—** I, Shyam Lall, being under the influence of the fair-complexioned ones <note>referring to women</note>, am composing this book named "Stree Jataka." This is done after offering salutations to Lord Shri Govardhan Nath, the upholder of the Govardhan mountain, and joyfully bowing to my mother, named Gauri, and my father, named Baldev Prasad. ||1||

Now, therefore, I shall fully describe the faults of maidens, by knowing which a wise man may never sink into the ocean of sorrow. ||2||
Therefore, a prudent man should examine a maiden possessed of auspicious marks and marry her, so that he does not become a vessel of misfortune in any way. ||3||
Since a virtuous wife is the means for achieving Virtue, Wealth, and Desire <note>The three worldly goals of human life: Dharma, Artha, and Kama</note>, I shall therefore describe the characteristics of women and their birth charts. ||4||

**Meaning—** Following this, I describe the countless faults of maidens. By knowing these traits, no wise man will have to bathe in the ocean of misery. ||2|| For this reason, one should test a maiden endowed with specific characteristics so that by marrying her, one does not always remain a partner to suffering. ||3|| For a faithful wife is the means to achieve religious merit and wealth...

<summary>This opening page introduces the "Stree Jataka," a classical text on female astrology and physiognomy, where the author Shyam Lall outlines his lineage and the importance of evaluating a bride's traits to ensure a prosperous marriage.</summary>
<keywords>Stree Jataka, astrology, female horoscopy, marriage, Shyam Lall, Govardhan, virtuous wife, Hindu astrology</keywords>

<page-num>2</page-num>
# Female Horoscopy

Since the fulfillment of desires and other fruits of life are achieved through men, we shall now discuss the physical characteristics and the rising signs <note>Lagna</note> of women. ||4||

->Now, the Sage Vyasa describes the specific physical traits of women:<-

A woman with eyes as yellow as a cat's is known as a "Poison-Maiden" <term>Vishakanya: a woman believed to bring misfortune or death to her husband</term>. Other teachers have said that a woman with eyes as yellow as gold is not exceedingly wicked, but rather of middling character. ||5||

A woman with a black tongue, long lips, yellow-colored eyes, and a hoarse or rattling voice should be avoided. Likewise, one who has hair growing on her feet, breasts, or lips must be rejected. ||6||

A woman whose fingers or teeth are widely spaced, who has excessive hair on her breasts or cheeks, or who possesses a black palate should be shunned. This also applies to those who resemble their parents too closely or possess deformed or extra limbs. ||7||

If a woman's little toe, ring toe, or middle toe does not touch the ground while walking, she should be known as an adulteress—that is, one who goes after other men. ||8||

<note>original: "स्त्रीजातक" (Strijataka) - Female Horoscopy</note>
<note>original: "विषकन्या" (Vishakanya) - Poison-Maiden</note>
<note>original: "व्यास" (Vyasa) - A revered ancient sage and author</note>
<note>original: "लक्षण" (Lakshana) - Physical traits or bodily marks</note>
<note>original: "व्यभिचारिणी" (Vyabhicharini) - An unchaste woman or adulteress</note>
<note>original: "पिंगला" (Pingala) - Tawny, reddish-brown, or yellow-eyed</note>
<note>original: "त्याज्या" (Tyajya) - To be abandoned or rejected</note>

<summary>This page details physiognomic traits in women that were traditionally viewed as auspicious or inauspicious, specifically linking physical features like eye color and toe placement to moral character and the fate of the husband.</summary>
<keywords>Female Horoscopy, Physiognomy, Vishakanya, Vyasa, Astrological Omens, Physical Traits, Marriage Suitability</keywords>

--- Page 27 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of feminine physiognomy (Samudrika Shastra), focusing on physical traits and their perceived influence on character and family destiny.</meta>

<page-num>3</page-num>
<header>With Hindi Commentary</header>

->If the second toe of a woman's foot exceeds the big toe in length,<-
->she will not remain in her husband's house, being independent and following her own desires. ||9||<-
->A long belly destroys the father-in-law; a long forehead destroys the brother-in-law.<-
->Long buttocks destroy the husband, and a belly shaped like a tortoise carries away wealth. ||10||<-
->A hair-whorl on the back destroys the husband; a hair-whorl at the navel marks a faithful wife.<-
->A hair-whorl on the waist indicates an independent woman; one on the shoulder brings a share of wealth. ||11||<-
->She who has a pleasant voice, wears fine dress, has a soft body, and speaks sweetly,<-
->who has a graceful gait and is pleasing to the eyes and mind—such a maiden is highly praised. ||12||<-

**Meaning—** The woman whose toe next to the big toe <note>the index toe or *pradeshini*</note> is longer than the big toe does not stay in her husband's home; she acts according to her own will and is a "free-walker" <note>original: *kaamcharini*, implying one who follows her own whims or desires</note>. ||9|| And the woman whose stomach is long <note>protruding or elongated</note> brings ruin to her father-in-law; the woman whose forehead is long <note>unusually high or prominent</note> brings ruin to her husband's younger brother <note>the *devar*</note>. The woman whose pelvic region <note>or buttocks</note> is pendulous or long brings ruin to her husband, and the woman whose stomach is shaped like a tortoise <note>rounded and hard</note> destroys wealth. ||10|| The woman who has a whorl of hair <note>*romavali chakra*</note> on her back destroys her husband; the woman who has a hair-whorl in her navel is a devoted and faithful wife <note>*pativrata*</note>. The woman who has a hair-whorl on her waist acts according to her own desires, and the woman who has a hair-whorl on her shoulder is one who enjoys wealth. ||11|| The woman whose voice is beautiful, who wears fine clothes, has a delicate body, speaks sweet words, possesses a graceful gait, and whose very sight pleases the eyes and the mind—such a woman is exceedingly excellent. ||12||

# Female Horoscopy
<page-num>4</page-num>

->**The Female Horoscope**<-

A woman whose belly is shaped like that of a frog <note>original: "Manduka-kukshika"; implying a specific rounded, slightly distended shape considered auspicious</note> and is as well-proportioned as the circumference of a Banyan tree <note>original: "Nyagrodha-parimandala"; referring to the symmetrical and expansive shade of the sacred fig tree</note>—such a woman gives birth to a son who shall surely become a king. ||13||

The line which rises from the wrist and travels across the palm of a woman's hand until it reaches the middle finger <note>The middle finger is known as the finger of Saturn in Western palmistry; here it is called "Madhyama"</note>—if such a line is found on the palm of a woman, she becomes a great queen. If that same line is found rising on the palm of a man, it brings him the happiness and status of a king. ||14||

Furthermore, the line that originates at the base of the little finger <note>the "Kanishthika"</note> and extends into the space between the index finger <note>the "Pradeshini"</note> and the middle finger—this line determines the maximum lifespan. If it is long, she will live a full life <note>traditionally interpreted here as nearly 120 years</note>; if the line is deficient or broken, the lifespan is reduced accordingly. ||15||

At the base of the thumb are the lines of progeny. The thick, large lines indicate sons, while the thin, slender lines indicate daughters. Those lines that are unbroken and long signify children who will live a long life; those that are broken or short signify a brief lifespan for the offspring. ||16||

**Meaning—** A woman whose womb/belly is like a frog's and has a great expanse at the top like a Banyan tree gives birth to a son who becomes a king. ||13|| A woman who has a line in her palm reaching from the wrist to the middle finger will be a great queen, and if a man has that same line, he will be a king. ||14|| And a woman in whose hand a line is born from the root of the little finger and goes to the interval between the middle finger and the index finger, that woman will have a lifespan of slightly less than one hundred and twenty (120) years. ||15|| And if the lines born at the base of a woman's thumb are very large, she is a <term>Giver of Sons</term> <note>original: "Putradatri"</note>; if they are small and fine, they represent daughters. And if those same lines are without... <note>The text cuts off here, likely continuing to describe unbroken lines on the next page.</note>

<summary>This page details specific physical marks and palm lines (Samudrika Shastra) that predict a woman's destiny, the royal status of her children, her own longevity, and the gender of her offspring.</summary>

If the line is long and unbroken, there will be long-lived sons. If it is broken, the sons will be short-lived. If that same line is thin and long, the daughter will be long-lived, and if it is broken, the daughter will be short-lived. ||16||

->The friendship between husband and wife should be considered in the space between the Life Line and the little finger; in the right hand for men and the left hand for women.<- <note>original: "कलत्रकांतयोः सख्यं..." - This verse establishes the gender-specific rules for reading palms in traditional Indian astrology.</note> ||17||

->Those men whose palms are either without lines or covered in too many lines are short-lived, destitute, and miserable; of this there is no doubt.<- ||18||

->A line resembling a leaf <note>original: "क्लेशसंपल्लवो" - likely referring to a frayed or feathered appearance</note> brings distress; a broken line causes a decrease in lifespan. Lines facing the wrist lead to growth, while those facing the thumb lead to misfortune.<- ||19||

->He who has a fish-shaped mark on his palm possesses great wealth. A fate line with a sharp tip and the shape of an umbrella is auspicious.<- ||20||

->If the spaces between the fingers are tightly closed, it leads to the accumulation of wealth. If there are gaps between them, they indicate a charitable or spendthrift nature.<- ||21||

**Meaning**—The line indicating mutual love between husband and wife is located between the Life Line and the little finger <term>kanishthika</term> <note>The smallest finger</note>. This line should be observed in the right hand of a man and the left hand of a woman. ||17|| Furthermore, those men or women who have no lines at all on their hands, or conversely, have too many lines, should be said to have a short lifespan; they remain poor and unhappy, there is no doubt in this. ||18|| And those whose hands have leaf-like lines facing the wrist <term>manibandha</term> <note>The junction of the hand and the forearm</note> are destined for hardship and sorrow. If a line is broken, it diminishes the lifespan. Lines directed toward the wrist signify prosperity, while lines directed toward the thumb signify calamities. ||19|| And the living being in whose palm there is a line resembling a fish <term>matsya-rekha</term> <note>A highly auspicious symbol in Indian palmistry signifying wealth and spiritual merit</note> becomes very wealthy. And in whose hand there is a sharp line near the wrist in the shape of an umbrella...

# Female Horoscopy

That line is named the Fate Line <note>original: "Bhagyarekha"</note>; it is considered excellent. ||20|| Furthermore, a woman whose fingers are closely joined together at the base, without gaps, accumulates wealth. Conversely, she who has gaps between her fingers spends wealth; money does not remain with her. ||21||

->A woman whose eyebrows meet, who is blind in one eye, has long lips, or ears shaped like winnowing baskets;<-
->one with a crooked mouth or nose, or who is either excessively silent or excessively talkative, should be avoided. ||22||<-
->She whose touch of hair or clothing causes flower garlands to wither, and in whose bathwater many tiny creatures perish;<-
->she whose clothes are infested with bedbugs and lice, who steals and eats food in secret, and who lacks cleanliness—such a woman must be rejected. ||24||<-

->Thus ends the first chapter, titled "Description of the Characteristics of Women," in the Female Horoscopy compiled by Pandit Shyamlal, the Royal Astrologer, son of Shri Baldev Prasad, the ornament of the Gauda lineage residing in Vanshavareli. ||1||<-

**Meaning—**A woman whose eyebrows are joined together or who is one-eyed; one whose lips are unusually long and whose ears are large like a grain-shuffling basket <note>original: "shurpa-karna"; a traditional flat basket used to toss grain</note>; one whose mouth or nose is crooked; or one who remains entirely silent or speaks too much—such a woman should be shunned in every way. ||22|| A woman whose hair or clothing is so harsh that touching them causes flower garlands to wilt, and one whose bathing water results in the death of many small insects <note>This implies a lack of spiritual merit or a "heavy" constitution</note> ||23||; and a woman whose hair and clothes are full of lice, who eats food stealthily (stealing it), and who is devoid of hygiene—that woman must certainly be rejected. ||24||

->Thus ends the first chapter, titled "Description of the Characteristics of Women," in the 'Shyamsundari' Hindi commentary written by Pandit Shyamlal, the Royal Astrologer, son of Shri Baldev Prasad, the ornament of the Gauda lineage residing in Vanshavareli. ||1||<-

# Now, from the Kashi Khanda within the Skanda Purana
## Regarding the specific characteristics of women

->Now, the reason for describing the characteristics of women is stated:<-
->Skanda spoke:<-

> If a woman possesses auspicious characteristics, the householder always enjoys happiness. Therefore, for the sake of the prosperity of happiness, one should first examine these characteristics. ||1||

Meaning—Lord Skanda says, "O Agastya! In the home of a householder where there is a woman with good qualities, that man always experiences joy. For this reason, for a person desiring the attainment of happiness, I shall first describe the characteristics of women." ||1||

### Now, the eightfold basis of characteristics is stated

> Body, hair-whirls, scent, shadow, character, voice, gait, and color—these are the eight bases of characteristics declared by the wise. ||2||

Meaning—<term>Body</term> <note>Vapu: the physical build</note>, <term>Whirls</term> <note>Avarta: circular hair patterns or skin marks</note>, <term>Scent</term> <note>Gandha: natural body odor</note>, <term>Shadow</term> <note>Chaya: the luster or aura</note>, <term>Character</term> <note>Sattva: inner strength or truthfulness; the original Sanskrit uses 'Sattva' while the commentary uses 'Parakrama' or valor</note>, <term>Voice</term> <note>Svara: tone and pitch</note>, <term>Gait</term> <note>Gati: the manner of walking</note>, and <term>Color</term> <note>Varna: complexion</note>. These eight types of foundational characteristics have been described by scholars. ||2||

### Now, the method of examination

> Starting from the soles of the feet and proceeding up to the hair on the head, I shall describe the auspicious and inauspicious signs in order; listen, O Sage! ||3||

Meaning—Starting from the soles of the feet and moving in sequence up to the hair of the head, I shall tell you the good and bad characteristics of a woman. O Agastya! Listen. ||3||

### Now, the sequence of characteristics

First the soles of the feet, the lines, then the big toes, the fingers, and the nails.

**Meaning—** Of both cheeks (49); of the mouth (50); of both lips (51); of the teeth (52)...

<meta>This page continues the systematic cataloging of feminine physical traits, transitioning from the introductory list of body parts to specific anatomical divinations starting with the soles of the feet.</meta>

<page-num>9</page-num>
<header>With Hindi Commentary</header>

The introduction describes the characteristics of sixty-six parts of a woman's body: the tongue (53), the uvula (54), the palate (55), the smile (56), the nose (57), the sneeze (58), the eyes (59), the eyelids (60), the eyebrows (61), the ears (62), the forehead (63), the head (64), the parting of the hair (65), and the hair itself (66). These conclude the introductory section. ||8-9||

->Now, the characteristics of the soles of the feet are described<-

> *The soles of a woman's feet should be smooth, fleshy, soft, even, free from sweat, warm, and reddish in hue; such feet are considered worthy of great enjoyment.* ||10|| <note>original Sanskrit: "strīṇāṃ pādatalaṃ snigdhaṃ māṃsalaṃ mṛdulaṃ samam..."</note>
> *Soles that are dry, discolored, rough, cracked, uneven, shaped like a winnowing basket, or excessively withered indicate sorrow and misfortune.* ||11||

Meaning—A woman whose soles are smooth, well-fleshed, soft, symmetrical, without perspiration, warm, and possess a reddish tint is destined for a life of great pleasure and luxury. ||10|| Conversely, a woman whose soles are dry, cracked, harsh, or broken—such that her footprints appear fragmented on the earth—or are wide at the front like a <term>winnowing basket (shurpa)</term> and especially withered, brings about sorrow and poverty. ||11||

->Now, the characteristics of the lines on the soles are described<-

> *She who possesses marks on her soles resembling a wheel, a swastika, a conch, a lotus, a flag, a fish, or an umbrella shall become the consort of a king.* ||12|| <note>original Sanskrit: "cakrasvastikaśaṅkhābja..."</note>
> *A continuous vertical line reaching the middle toe signifies uninterrupted enjoyment. Lines resembling a rat, a serpent, or a crow indicate sorrow and poverty.* ||13||

Meaning—A woman who has marks on the soles of her feet resembling a <term>wheel (chakra)</term>, a <term>swastika</term> <note>an ancient symbol of auspiciousness and well-being</note>, a <term>conch (shankha)</term>, a <term>lotus (kamal)</term>, a <term>banner (dhvaja)</term>, a <term>fish (meen)</term>, or a <term>royal umbrella (chhatra)</term> will become a queen. ||12|| Furthermore, a woman who has an unbroken vertical line extending to the middle toe of her foot is excellent for the enjoyment of life's pleasures...

<header>The Nativity of Women</header>

And she whose feet possess lines resembling a rat, a serpent, or a crow, such a woman brings about sorrow and poverty. ||13||

### Now, the characteristics of the big toe are described:

**A big toe that is elevated, fleshy, and rounded bestows immense enjoyment.**
**One that is crooked, short, or flat destroys happiness and good fortune. ||14||**
**A very wide big toe leads to widowhood, and a long one brings ill-fortune.**

Meaning—If a woman's big toe is high, fleshy, and round, then that woman provides great happiness. If a woman's big toe is crooked, short, and flat, she is a destroyer of good fortune. ||14|| Furthermore, a woman with an excessively wide big toe becomes a widow, and one with a long big toe is considered unfortunate <note>original: "durbhaga," referring to one who is not favored or lacks grace</note>.

### Now, the characteristics of the toes are described:

**Soft toes are praised, as are those that are dense, rounded, and elevated. ||15||**

Meaning—The maiden whose toes are soft, closely set, round, excellent, and high is an auspicious woman. ||15||

**With long toes, she becomes unchaste; with thin toes, extremely poor.**
**With short toes, she has a short lifespan; with bent toes, she follows a crooked path. ||16||**
**With flat toes, she becomes a servant; with widely spaced toes, she is impoverished.**
**If the toes are found to be overlapping one another, ||17||**
**Then, even after losing many husbands, she becomes a servant to others.**
**She who, while walking on the path, causes the dust of the earth to rise up, ||18||**
**She is known as a "dust-raiser" <note>original: "pamsula," a term for an unchaste woman</note> and brings destruction to three lineages.**
**She whose little toe does not touch the ground while walking, ||19||**
**That woman, after the death of her husband, takes a second husband.**

# With Hindi Commentary

->The woman whose ring finger or middle finger does not touch the ground, ||20||<-
->The first (ring finger) destroys two husbands, and the second (middle finger) destroys three.<-
->If both of these are short or missing, they cause the state of being without a husband. ||21||<-
->She whose forefinger <note>The toe next to the big toe</note> exceeds the big toe in length,<-
->Even as a maiden, she will be unchaste; this fault is certain. ||22||<-

**Meaning**—A woman whose toes are excessively long becomes unchaste <note>original: "kulata," referring to a woman who brings dishonor to her family</note>, while one with very thin toes will be destitute. One with very short toes has a short lifespan, and one with toes of uneven sizes (some small, some large) will be a deceptive procuress <note>original: "kutini," a woman who acts as a go-between in illicit affairs</note>. ||16|| Furthermore, a woman with flat toes becomes a servant, and one with gaps between her toes becomes impoverished. A woman whose toes overlap one another ||17|| will cause the death of many husbands and become a procuress for other women. A woman who kicks up a great deal of dust from the earth while walking ||18|| is like a promiscuous courtesan; she destroys the three lineages of her father, her maternal grandfather, and her husband. A woman whose little toe <term>kanishthika: the fifth and smallest digit</term> does not touch the ground while walking ||19|| will cause the death of her wedded husband and take another. A woman whose ring finger <term>anamika: the fourth digit</term> does not touch the ground ||20|| will destroy her first two husbands and take a third. A woman whose middle finger <term>madhya: the third digit</term> does not touch the ground will kill three husbands and take a fourth. If both the little toe and the ring toe are deficient or short, that woman will remain without a husband entirely. ||21|| Finally, if the toe next to the big toe <term>pradeshini: the second toe or fore-toe</term> is longer than the big toe, that woman—even if unmarried—will certainly become unchaste. ||22||

<header>The Female Horoscope <note>original: "Stri-Jatakam"; a treatise on the physical characteristics and destinies of women</note></header>

### Now, the characteristics of the toenails are described

Toenails that are glossy, elevated, copper-colored <note>indicating a healthy, reddish tint</note>, and rounded are auspicious.
**Meaning:** A woman whose toenails are smooth, raised, reddish in hue, and circular possesses nails that bring good fortune.

### Now, the characteristics of the top of the foot are described

An elevated instep on a woman's foot indicates she will be a queen. ||23||
One that is free of sweat, devoid of visible veins, smooth, soft, and fleshy is favorable.
One that is sunken in the middle indicates poverty, and one covered in veins indicates a woman who is always wandering <note>literally "always on the road," implying a life of toil or lack of a settled home</note>. ||24||
**Meaning:** A woman whose instep <note>the "back" or top of the foot</note> is high and well-arched becomes the wife of a king. ||23|| Furthermore, a woman whose instep does not sweat, shows no protruding veins, and is soft, smooth, and well-fleshed is considered auspicious. Conversely, a woman whose instep is hollow or "broken" in the center will face poverty, and she whose instep is thick with visible veins will be destined to wander constantly. ||24||

### Now, the characteristics of the ankle joints are described

If the ankles are covered in thick hair, she will be a servant; if they are fleshless <note>bony</note>, she will be unfortunate.
Hidden ankle-bones <note>covered by firm flesh</note> are said to be for the sake of well-being, provided they are without visible veins and well-rounded. ||25||
Ankles that are excessively large, thick, or loose and prominent are indicators of ill fortune.
**Meaning:** A woman whose ankle joints <note>term: "Gulpha" - the malleolus or ankle bone</note> are very hairy is destined for servitude, and she whose ankles lack flesh is unlucky. Ankles that are concealed by flesh, free of veins, and perfectly round are auspicious. ||25|| However, if the ankles are overly large, swollen, or appear loose and bony, that woman is indicated to have a difficult or unfortunate life.

<summary>This page details the physiognomic signs of the toenails, instep, and ankles, correlating physical traits like smoothness and "hidden" bones with royalty and prosperity, while visible veins or hair signify poverty and labor.</summary>
<keywords>physiognomy, Samudrika Shastra, toenails, instep, ankles, divination, feminine traits, social status</keywords>

## Now, the characteristics of the back of the foot (the heel) are described:

**A woman with even heels is auspicious; one with thick heels is unfortunate. ||26||**
**One with raised heels may be unchaste, while one with long heels suffers grief. ||**

Meaning—A woman whose heels <note>original: "paṣṇi"</note> are even and symmetrical is considered auspicious. If the heels are overly thick or fleshy, she is considered ill-fated or unfortunate ||26||. A woman whose heels are raised or high-arched is said to be unchaste <note>original: "kulata," referring to a woman who leaves her family or is of loose character</note>, and if the heels are excessively long, she experiences much sorrow in life.

---

## Now, the characteristics of the calves are described:

**She whose calves are hairless, even, smooth, and gradually rounded ||27||**
**becomes a queen, provided they are beautiful and without visible veins. ||28||**
**One hair per follicle makes a queen; two hairs bring happiness.**
**Three hairs in the pores leads to the sorrow of widowhood. ||29||**

Meaning—A woman whose calves <note>original: "pindli"</note> are hairless, smooth, and perfectly rounded ||27|| becomes the wife of a king. If the calves are beautiful and free of protruding veins, she is considered excellent ||28||. If there is only one hair emerging from each pore, she becomes a queen. If there are two hairs, she enjoys a life of comfort. However, if there are three hairs emerging from a single pore <note>original: "romakupa"</note>, she is destined to suffer the grief of widowhood ||29||.

---

## Now, the characteristics of the knees are described:

**A pair of knees that are round and well-covered with flesh is praised.**
**Fleshless knees belong to an independent woman; those that are shaky belong to the poor. ||30||**

Meaning—A woman whose two knees <note>original: "janu"</note> are round and well-padded with flesh is considered excellent. If the knees are devoid of flesh (bony), that woman is "swairini" <note>meaning a woman who acts of her own free will, often used here to imply a lack of traditional restraint</note>, that is—

# Female Horoscopy
<note>original: "Stri-jātakam" — a branch of astrology focusing on the physical traits and fates of women.</note>

...she becomes unchaste, and she whose knees are loose or weak becomes impoverished. || 30 ||

### Now, the Characteristics of the Shins
<note>original: "Janghā-lakshanam"</note>

With shins free of visible veins, shaped like a camel's back <note>referring to a smooth, curved slope</note>, smooth, thick, well-rounded, and hairless, women become the beloved of kings. || 31 ||

Hairy shins are said to bring widowhood, while flat shins bring misfortune. Shins that have a gap between them when standing cause great sorrow, and those with tough, coarse skin lead to poverty. || 32 ||

**Meaning—** A woman whose shins are devoid of prominent veins, shaped like the back of a camel or the trunk of an elephant <note>implying a graceful taper</note>, touching each other without gaps, perfectly rounded, and hairless, becomes a queen or a king's favorite. || 31 || If the shins are covered in hair, she becomes a widow. Those with flattened shins are ill-fated, those with a hollow space between the shins suffer great distress, and a woman with hard, leathery skin on her shins remains in a state of poverty. || 32 ||

### Now, the Characteristics of the Waist
<note>original: "Kati-lakshanam"</note>

A waist measuring twenty-four fingers in circumference is praised; for deer-eyed women, a waist that is elevated, possessing ample hips, and well-proportioned is auspicious. || 33 ||

A waist that is sunken, flat, long, fleshless, or cramped—as well as a short or hairy waist—indicates sorrow and widowhood for a woman. || 34 ||

**Meaning—** A woman whose waist is twenty-four fingers <term>angula: a traditional unit of measurement based on finger-width</term> in extent, featuring high, ample buttocks and a broad, square-set frame, is considered fortunate. || 33 || Conversely, a waist that is slumped, flat, overly long, lacking flesh, stiff, unusually short, or covered in hair indicates a woman who will endure suffering or become a widow. || 34 ||

### Now, the Characteristics of the Buttocks
<note>original: "Nitamba-lakshanam"</note>

The orb of the buttocks in women should be elevated, fleshy, and broad...

## Now, the characteristics of the vulva <term>yoni: the female reproductive organ</term> are described:

A vulva that resembles the back of a tortoise or the shoulder of an elephant is auspicious. If the left side is raised, a daughter is born; if the right side is raised, a son. ||37||
One with sparse hair, a hidden clitoris <note>original: "gūḍhamaṇi"; literally 'hidden jewel'</note>, well-formed, compact, wide, high, and the color of a lotus, shaped like a Peepal leaf, is auspicious. ||38||

She whose vulva is shaped like a spiral shell <note>original: "shaṅkhāvartta"</note> does not desire pregnancy in this world. One that is flat or shaped like a potsherd brings the status of a servant-maid. ||40||
One resembling a bamboo or rattan leaf, with hair like an elephant's and a high "nose," or one that is hideous, crooked in shape, or has hanging "cheeks" <note>referring to the labia majora</note> is likewise inauspicious. ||41||

The forehead of the vulva—

**Meaning—** The woman whose vulva is like the back of a tortoise or the shoulders of an elephant...

# Female Nativity

<page-num>16</page-num>

If the <term>yoni</term><note>The female reproductive organ, often used in these texts to denote both external and internal anatomy</note> is symmetrical and elevated on the left side, that woman will give birth to female children. If the aforementioned specific anatomy is elevated on the right side, that woman will give birth to strong, healthy offspring. ||37|| Furthermore, the girl whose anatomy has hair like that of a mouse, with hidden joints, and is beautiful, firm, thick, elevated, and shaped like a lotus petal or a sacred fig leaf, is considered auspicious. ||38|| An anatomy shaped like a deer's hoof or a hearth, covered in excessive hair, or with a protrusion shaped like the mouth of a water pot, is considered extremely inauspicious. ||39||

A woman whose anatomy has folds like a conch shell will not conceive, and a woman with a flat anatomy, shaped like a shard of pottery, becomes a <term>duti</term><note>A female messenger or go-between, often implying a woman of loose character or a procuress in this context</note>. ||40|| And the woman whose anatomy is shaped like a bamboo leaf, with hair like an elephant's, and which is high-bridged, terrifying, crooked, long, and narrow-necked, is inauspicious. Such a forehead of the anatomy is said to be undesirable. ||41||

->Now, the characteristics of the hips and pelvis are described<-

>—The hips should be broad, elevated, and fleshy.
>Soft, rich with soft hair, and turning toward the right. ||42||
>Turning toward the left, lacking flesh, or being crooked indicates widowhood.
>Hips that are narrow, sunken, or dry always bring sorrow. ||43||
<note>original Sanskrit: "जघनं विस्तीर्णं तुङ्गमांसलम्... जघनं दुःखदं सदा"</note>

**Meaning**—The woman whose <term>jaghana</term><note>The hips, loins, or pelvic region</note> is broad, elevated, fleshy, and endowed with soft, delicate hair, and which curves or whorls toward the right side, is auspicious. If the whorl is toward the left and the area is devoid of flesh, it is crooked and indicates she will become a widow. She whose pelvic structure is constricted and dry always experiences suffering. ||42||43||

### Now, the Characteristics of the Pelvis

->A pelvis is praised when it is wide, soft, and slightly elevated.<-
->One that is hairy, veiny, or marked with lines is not auspicious. || 44 ||<-

Meaning—The part below the navel is called the <term>Basti</term><note>The pelvic region or lower abdomen</note>. A woman whose pelvic region is expansive, soft, and slightly raised is said to be auspicious. Conversely, a pelvic region covered in hair or marked with prominent lines makes a woman inauspicious. || 44 ||

---

### Now, the Characteristics of the Navel

->A deep navel that spirals to the right brings happiness and wealth.<-
->One that spirals to the left, is shallow, or has a visible knot is not auspicious. || 45 ||<-

Meaning—A woman whose <term>Tudi</term><note>A colloquial term for the navel or umbilicus</note> is deep and <term>Dakshinavarta</term><note>original: "दक्षिणावर्त्ता" - spiraling clockwise or to the right</note> is a provider of happiness and prosperity. One whose navel spirals to the left, is raised/protruding, or has a knot-like appearance is inauspicious. || 45 ||

---

### Now, the Varieties of Characteristics of the Belly

->A woman with a broad belly gives birth to many sons and is an abode of happiness.<-
->With a belly shaped like a frog, she shall give birth to a son who is a lord of the earth. || 46 ||<-
->With a belly that is elevated, wrinkled, or spiraled,<-
->A woman here becomes, in order: barren, an ascetic, or a servant. || 47 ||<-

Meaning—A woman who possesses heavy or broad <term>Kukshi</term><note>The belly, womb, or flanks</note> gives birth to sons and provides great comfort. One whose womb/belly is shaped like that of a frog will have a son who becomes a king <note>original: "क्षितीशं" - lord of the earth</note>. || 46 || Furthermore, a woman with a high or protruding belly is barren; one with a belly marked by heavy folds or wrinkles becomes a female ascetic; and a woman with a spiraled or twisted waist/belly becomes a servant. These should be understood in that specific order. || 47 ||

## Now, the characteristics of the flanks are described

If a woman’s flanks <note>the sides of the body between the ribs and hips</note> are even, well-fleshed, soft, and have bones that are well-concealed, they are auspicious. Such a woman is undoubtedly a treasury of good fortune and happiness. (48)

She whose flanks show visible veins, are overly elevated, and are covered in hair, will be childless, of poor character, and a vessel of constant sorrow. (49)

**Meaning**—A woman whose ribs are even, fleshy, soft, and have bones that appear hidden is auspicious; she is undoubtedly a place of good fortune and wealth. (48) And she in whose ribs the veins are visible, which are high on both sides and possess hair, that woman is without offspring, of a wicked nature, and a collection of miseries. (49)

## Now, the characteristics of the abdomen

With a very slender abdomen that is free of visible veins and has soft skin, a woman becomes rich in enjoyments and always partakes in sweet foods. (50)

A belly shaped like a pot <note>original: "kumbhakaram"</note> belongs to a woman in poverty. One shaped like a drum <note>a mridangam</note>, or like a pumpkin, or like a grain of barley, is difficult to satisfy and brings ill-fortune to a woman. (51)

A woman with a very large abdomen is childless and unfortunate. One with a pendulous or sagging belly destroys <note>brings ill luck to</note> her father-in-law and her husband's younger brother. (52)

She whose waist is thin is fortunate and wealthy in pleasures, possessing three folds of skin <note>term: "vali-traya" - three horizontal folds on the abdomen, considered a mark of extreme beauty in classical India</note>. She whose line of hair <note>term: "romali" - the fine line of hair leading to the navel</note> is straight and thin is a source of joy and playfulness. (53)

A line of hair that is brownish-red, crooked, thick, or broken brings about thievery, widowhood, and misfortune for women in this world. (54)

**Meaning**—A woman whose stomach is small, devoid of prominent veins, and possesses soft skin is fit for the enjoyment of pleasures and daily eats sweet delicacies. (50) And she whose stomach is like a water-pot...

A woman becomes impoverished [if her abdomen is misshapen], and she whose belly is shaped like a *mridanga* <note>a barrel-shaped drum</note>, an axe, or a grain of barley is filled with great sorrow. (51) Furthermore, a woman with a very large belly is childless and unfortunate <note>original: "durbhaga," meaning one who is not loved by her husband or lacks good fortune</note>. A woman with an exceptionally long and wide belly brings destruction to her father-in-law or her husband's younger brother. (52) Conversely, she whose belly is slender in the middle is a woman of superior fortune, and she who has three folds <note>original: "valaya," skin folds on the abdomen considered a sign of beauty and status</note> on her belly is destined for a life of enjoyment. She who has a straight, fine line of hair on her belly is a giver of all auspiciousness. (53) However, a woman who has a yellowish, slanted, or broken line of hair on her belly is a thief, a widow, and possesses a wicked destiny. (54)

->Now, the characteristics of the heart/chest are described:<-

The woman whose chest is hairless, even, and free from depressions attains sovereignty, avoids widowhood, and receives the beloved's affection. (55)
A woman with an overly broad chest is unchaste and merciless. One whose chest has protruding hair certainly destroys her husband. (56)
A chest that is eighteen fingers wide, firm, and elevated leads to happiness; if it is hairy, uneven, or excessively thick, it leads to sorrow. (57)

**Meaning—** The woman whose heart <note>referring here to the chest area</note> is devoid of hair, level, and without hollows is endowed with prosperity, is dear to her husband, and is devoted to her husband's love. (55) A woman with a very wide chest is an adulteress <note>original: "punshchali," a woman who runs after men</note> and is devoid of mercy; she who has many coarse hairs <note>original: "rugaton," referring to small, stiff body hairs</note> on her heart/chest destroys her husband. (56) Furthermore, the woman whose chest is eighteen fingers wide <term>Ashtadashangula: a traditional measurement where one 'angula' is the width of a finger</term>, firm, and high is a giver of happiness. But the woman whose chest is uneven and covered in coarse hair is a giver of sorrow. (57)

### Now, the characteristics of the breasts are described

->Breasts that are dense, round, firm, plump, and symmetrical are praised.<-
->Those with thick tips, widely separated, or withered do not bring happiness to beautiful women. || 58 ||<-
->A woman whose right breast is more elevated is considered the foremost among those who bear sons.<-
->One whose left breast is more elevated gives birth to daughters and possesses the beauty of good fortune. || 59 ||<-
->Breasts shaped like the jars of a water-wheel <note>original: "arghatta-ghati"; referring to the pots on a Persian wheel used for irrigation</note> indicate a lack of character.<-
->Those with bloated tips, a wide gap between them, or excessively broad bases are not auspicious. || 60 ||<-
->Breasts thick at the base, gradually tapering, and sharp at the tip,<-
->bring happiness in the early years but extreme sorrow later in life. || 61 ||<-

**Meaning—** The woman whose breasts are dense, round, firm, and equal to one another is considered excellent. Conversely, breasts that are thick at the front, widely spaced, or withered are not favorable for women. || 58 || Furthermore, the woman whose right breast is higher will give birth to sons, while she whose left breast is higher will give birth to daughters and be blessed with the beauty of good fortune. || 59 || A woman whose breasts resemble the water-jars of an irrigation wheel is of poor character <note>original: "kushila"; meaning one with bad conduct or morals</note>. Breasts that have thick tips, are far apart, or are excessively large at the bottom are undesirable. || 60 || Finally, if a woman's breasts are thick at the base and gradually become thin toward the top with sharp tips, she experiences happiness in her youth but suffers great misery in her later years. || 61 ||

### Now, the characteristics of the nipples are described

->For beautiful-eyed women, a pair of nipples that are dark and perfectly round is praised.<-
->Those that are sunken, elongated, or thin lead to a life of hardship. || 62 ||<-

**Meaning—** The woman whose nipples <term>chuchuka: the tip or nipple of the breast</term> are round with a darkish hue is...

is auspicious; if they are indented inward, weak, or long, they cause distress. (62)

### Now, the Characteristics of the Collarbones

->A woman with thick collarbones possesses treasures of wealth and grain.<-
->One with sunken, uneven, or protruding bones is impoverished. (63)<-

**Meaning—**A woman whose <term>collarbone joints</term><note>original: "jatra"</note> are thick and well-fleshed is a vessel of wealth and food. One with loose bones, sunken bones, or unevenly sized bones is destined for poverty. (63)

### Now, the Characteristics of the Shoulders

->Shoulders that are firm, not drooping, not overly long, and well-fleshed are auspicious.<-
->Crooked, thick, and excessively hairy shoulders indicate a servant or widowhood. (64)<-
->Shoulders with hidden joints and sloping tips are auspicious and well-knit.<-
->High-tipped shoulders bring widowhood; fleshless ones bring great sorrow. (65)<-

**Meaning—**If a woman’s shoulders are firm, not hanging low, and are thick rather than thin, they are excellent. If they are crooked, very thick, and covered in <term>fine hair</term><note>original: "rugte"</note>, she becomes a messenger or a widow. (64) Furthermore, a woman whose shoulders have hidden joints and are well-integrated with flesh is considered superior. Shoulders that rise too high toward the tips indicate widowhood, and fleshless or "hollow" shoulders bring suffering. (65)

### Now, the Characteristics of the Underarms

->Underarms with very fine hair, elevated, smooth, and fleshy are praised.<-
->Deep, veiny, and sweat-drenched underarms are not praised. (66)<-

**Meaning—**A woman whose underarms have fine hair, are fleshy, smooth, and slightly elevated is auspicious. Conversely, if those same areas are deep, filled with visible veins, or constantly oily with sweat, they are unfavorable. (66)

# Page 22
## Female Horoscopy
<note>original: "Stri-Jatakam" — a branch of astrology focusing on the life and character of women based on physical traits.</note>

### Now, the characteristics of the arms are described

> If the arms are free of defects, with hidden bones, soft joints,
> without visible veins or hair, and straight, they belong to deer-eyed women. ||67||
> Thick hair on the arms indicates widowhood; short arms indicate misfortune;
> and arms with visible veins bring great suffering to women. ||68||

**Meaning—** The arms of a woman are considered auspicious if they are free of defects, have hidden bones <note>meaning well-fleshed or plump enough that the skeletal structure is not prominent</note>, are soft, lack protruding joints, and are free of visible veins and hair ||67||. Conversely, a woman whose arms have thick hair becomes a widow; one whose arms are short is unfortunate; and a woman whose veins are visible on her arms experiences constant hardship ||68||.

### Now, the characteristics of the thumb and fingers are described

> Shaped like a lotus bud, with fingers and thumb aligned,
> such a pair of hands for deer-eyed women bestows many enjoyments. ||69||

**Meaning—** A woman whose fingers and thumb are straight and well-formed like the stalk of a lotus <note>original: "ambhoja-mukula" — specifically the tapering shape of a budding lotus</note> is worthy of great luxury and pleasures ||69||.

### Now, the characteristics of the palms are described

> Soft, raised in the center, reddish, and without gaps between fingers,
> such a palm is excellent, especially if marked with auspicious lines and few in number. ||70||
> Many lines lead to widowhood; no lines lead to poverty;
> and a palm full of visible veins makes a woman a beggar. ||71||

**Meaning—** The palm of a woman is auspicious if it is soft, raised in the middle, reddish in color, and has no gaps when the fingers are pressed together. It is considered excellent if it possesses noble marks <term>shastra-rekha</term> <note>lines resembling auspicious symbols like weapons or tools of status</note> and has relatively few lines ||70||. A woman with too many lines on her palm becomes a widow; one with no lines at all faces poverty; and a woman whose palms are covered in prominent veins becomes a beggar ||71||. Many...

If the hand is marked with [certain] lines, she becomes a widow; a maiden with lineless hands becomes impoverished; and a woman whose veins are visible on her hands becomes a beggar. ||71||

### Now, the characteristics of the back of the hand

> <note>original: "viloma viśiraṃ śastaṃ pāṇipṛṣṭhaṃ samunnatam | vaidhavyahetu romāḍhyaṃ nimnaṃ śirāyutaṃ tyajet ||72||"</note>
> The back of the hand that is hairless, free of visible veins, and well-elevated is considered auspicious. One should avoid a woman whose back of the hand is covered in hair, sunken, and veiny, as these are causes of widowhood. ||72||

**Meaning—** A woman whose back of the hand is free of hair <term>rugate: small hairs or bristles</term>, devoid of prominent veins, and raised/fleshy is auspicious. Conversely, a woman whose back of the hand is hairy, sunken, and veiny becomes a widow; such a woman should be avoided. ||72||

### Now, the characteristics of the lines of the hand

> <note>original: "raktā vyaktā gabhīrā ca snigdhā pūrṇā ca vartulā | kararekhāṅganāyā: syācchubhā bhāgyānusārataḥ ||73|| matsyena subhagā nārī svastvikena vasupradā | padmena bhūpaternārī janayedbhūpatiṃ sutam ||74|| cakravartistriyā: pāṇau nadyāvarta: pradakṣiṇa: | śaṃkhātapatrakamaṭhā nṛpamātṛtvasūcakā: ||75|| tulāmānākṛtī rekhā vaṇikpatnī tu sā bhavet | gajavājivṛṣākārā: kare vāme mṛgīdṛśām ||76||"</note>

**Meaning—** A woman in whose hand the lines are reddish in color, clearly visible, deep, smooth, complete (not broken), and rounded is auspicious according to her fate. ||73|| And she who has a line resembling a fish <term>matsya-rekha: a fish-shaped mark on the palm</term> becomes fortunate and well-loved. She who has a mark like a cross <note>original: "svastika"</note> becomes a giver of wealth. She who has a line like a lotus <note>original: "padma"</note> becomes the wife of a king and gives birth to a son who will be a king. ||74|| In the hand of the wife of a universal emperor <note>original: "chakravarti"</note>, there is a clockwise whirlpool mark <note>original: "nadyavarta"</note>. Marks of a conch, a royal parasol, or a tortoise indicate that she will be the mother of a king. ||75|| If the lines take the shape of a balance scale, she will be the wife of a merchant. If the shapes of an elephant, a horse, or a bull appear on the left hand of the deer-eyed women... ||76||

...should be; and she in whose hand there are lines resembling a trident <note>original: "trishula," the three-pronged spear of Shiva</note>, a mace, a sword, a spear, or a drum <note>original: "nagada," a large kettle-drum</note> ||78|| That woman, through her renunciation—that is, by giving charity—becomes greatly renowned upon the earth. ### Now, the characteristics of the thumb are described:

->A thumb that is straight, rounded, with circular nails, and soft is auspicious. ||81||<-

Meaning—Those whose hand thumbs are straight and round are auspicious. Furthermore, the thumbs of women that have circular nails and are soft are considered fortunate. ||81||

### Now, the characteristics of the fingers are described:

->Fingers with beautiful joints, long, rounded, and gradually tapering are auspicious.<-
->Flat, uneven, rough, or those with hair on the back are inauspicious. ||82||<-
->Very short, thin, crooked, widely spaced, or hairy fingers<-
->with many joints bring misery to women. ||83||<-

Meaning—Fingers with beautiful segments <note>original: "poru," the sections between joints</note>, which are round and gradually become slender toward the tips, are auspicious. Conversely, fingers that are flat, thick, dry, or have hair on the back are inauspicious ||82||. Additionally, fingers that are excessively short, thin, bent, hairy, widely spaced, or have too many knots/joints bring sorrow to women. ||83||

### Now, the characteristics of the fingernails are described:

->Reddish, peaked, and high nails are auspicious for beautiful-eyed women.<-
->Sunken, discolored, oyster-like, or yellow nails grant poverty. ||84||<-

> White spots on the nails usually belong to a woman who is independent and follows her own will. Even men born with such "flowered" nails suffer from sorrow. ||85||

**Meaning—** A woman with reddish, peaked, and high nails is auspicious. Women with soft, spreading, yellow nails like oyster shells are poor. ||84|| And those women who have white spots on their nails are often <term>svairini<note>A woman who acts according to her own whims, often implying a lack of traditional restraint.</note></term>, wandering as they please. Furthermore, men who have white spots or specks on their nails also experience suffering. ||85||

### Now, the characteristics of the back are described:

> A back where the spine is deeply set, fleshy, and firm is auspicious. With a hairy back, she certainly attains widowhood. ||86||
> With a crooked, bent, or veined back, she is sorrowful.

**Meaning—** A woman whose back is low in the center with a spine as straight as a bamboo stalk <note>original: "vamsa-asthi," literally bamboo-bone</note>, and whose back is well-fleshed and strong, is auspicious. A woman whose back is covered in hair becomes a widow. ||86|| And she whose back is crooked, sunken, or covered in visible veins is a woman who lives in distress.

### Now, the characteristics of the nape of the neck are described:

> A straight nape is best, fleshy and well-raised. ||87||
> If it is withered, veined, very hairy, broad, or crooked, it is inauspicious.

**Meaning—** A woman whose <term>krikatika<note>The joint of the neck or the nape.</note></term> is straight, fleshy, and elevated is considered excellent. ||87|| However, a woman whose nape is dry, veined, hairy, high, or crooked should be known as inauspicious.

### Now, the characteristics of the neck are described

A fleshy, well-rounded neck measuring four fingers <note>A traditional unit of measurement called an 'angula', roughly the width of a finger</note> in height is considered excellent. ||88||
A neck marked with three distinct lines <note>original: "trirekha," often compared to the lines on a conch shell, symbolizing beauty and fortune</note>, where the bones are not protruding and the structure is firm, is auspicious. A neck that is fleshless, flat, excessively long, or uneven is not a provider of good fortune. ||89||
A woman with a thick neck becomes a widow; one with a crooked neck becomes a servant; one with a flat neck is barren; and one with a short neck remains childless. ||90||

**Meaning—** The woman whose neck is fleshy, circular, and four fingers in height is auspicious. ||88|| And she whose throat is marked by three lines and has hidden bones is auspicious. The woman whose throat is devoid of flesh, flat, very long, or sunken is not auspicious. ||89|| Furthermore, the woman whose neck is thick becomes a widow, she with a crooked neck becomes a servant, she with a flattened neck is barren, and she with a short neck is without offspring. ||90||

### Now, the characteristics of the chin are described

A chin that is two fingers in width, well-rounded, plump, and very soft is excellent. One should avoid <note>meaning: consider inauspicious</note> a chin that is thick, cleft in two, elongated, or hairy. ||91||

**Meaning—** The chin of a woman that is two fingers wide, beautifully round, thick, and soft is auspicious. One that is overly stout, divided into two parts, wide, or covered in hair is inauspicious. ||91||

### Now, the characteristics of the jaw are described

The jaw <term>Hanu: the area of the jawbone connected to the chin</term> connected to the chin, which is hairless and well-proportioned, is auspicious. One that is crooked, thick, thin, short, or hairy does not provide good fortune. ||92||

**Meaning—** The area above the chin of a woman which is hairless and beautifully solid...

<summary>This page details the physiognomic interpretations of a woman's neck, chin, and jaw, correlating specific physical dimensions and textures with her future character and destiny. It emphasizes that rounded, fleshy, and well-proportioned features indicate good fortune, while hairiness or structural irregularities suggest hardship.</summary>

If it <note>referring to the neck or chin from the previous page</note> is so, it is auspicious; however, if it is crooked, thick, weak, short, or covered in hair, it is undesirable. ||92||

### Now, the Characteristics of the Cheeks

> The cheeks of a woman are praised if they are fleshy, round, and elevated. One should avoid those that are hairy, rough, sunken, or lacking in flesh. ||93||

Meaning—The woman whose cheeks are thick, round, and high possesses auspicious qualities. Conversely, cheeks that are covered in fine hair, harsh to the touch, sunken, or thin are considered undesirable. ||93||

### Now, the Characteristics of the Mouth

> A mouth that is even, fleshy, very smooth, fragrant, and circular—reflecting the likeness of her father’s face—belongs to those women who are blessed in this world. ||94||

Meaning—A woman whose mouth is well-fleshed, smooth, pleasant-smelling, circular in shape, and bears a resemblance to her father's countenance <note>original: "janitṛ-vadana-chāyam", implying a hereditary likeness to the progenitor</note> is considered truly fortunate and blessed in this world. ||94||

### Now, the Characteristics of the Lower Lip

> A lower lip that is pale red like a trumpet-flower, round, smooth, and adorned with lines in the center belongs to a woman who will be beloved by a king. ||95||
> A thin, protruding, cracked, or dry lip indicates misfortune. A dark-colored or thick lower lip belongs to one who may face widowhood or who is fond of quarreling. ||96||

Meaning—A woman whose lower lip is the color of a rose <note>original: "patala", the pale red of the Bignonia suaveolens</note>, smooth, round, and whose center is beautified by fine lines, becomes the beloved of a king <note>original: "dharājāni-priyā", literally 'dear to the lord of the earth'</note>. ||95|| On the other hand, thin, long, cracked (split), or dry lips are indicators of ill-fortune. Furthermore, a woman with yellowish-dark or thick lips is prone to widowhood and is someone who finds pleasure in conflict. ||96||

It emphasizes that features resembling the father or possessing "royal" qualities like fragrance and redness signify a fortunate life.</summary>
