## Now, the characteristics of the upper lip are described

> The upper lip of a charming woman, if smooth, brings great enjoyment. 
> Slightly raised in the middle and hairless, it is auspicious; the opposite brings ill effects. ||97||

**Meaning—** The woman whose upper lips are smooth and without visible veins <note>original: "nasorahit"</note> grants pleasure. One that is slightly elevated in the center and free of hair is auspicious. If the lip is the opposite of this, consider it undesirable. ||97||

---

## Now, the characteristics of the teeth are described

> Thirty-two teeth, glossy and resembling cow's milk in whiteness, are auspicious. 
> They should be even on the bottom and top, and only slightly prominent. ||98||
> Yellow or dark-colored teeth, or those in thick, long rows, 
> Or those shaped like oyster shells or widely spaced, are causes of grief and misfortune. ||99||
> With more teeth on the bottom than the top, she will surely consume <note>metaphorically: bring ruin to</note> her mother. 
> With hideous teeth, she becomes a widow; with widely spaced teeth, she becomes unchaste. ||100||

**Meaning—** A woman's thirty-two teeth are auspicious if they are glossy and white like cow's milk. If the upper teeth are even with the lower ones or just slightly higher, it is a good sign. ||98|| Teeth that are yellow-colored or dark like a monkey's <note>original: "bandar ke varna"</note>, or thick, long, in two rows, or shaped like shells and widely spaced—these types of teeth indicate sorrow and bad luck. ||99|| Furthermore, the woman whose lower teeth are more prominent than the upper ones brings destruction to her mother. A woman with cracked or hideous-looking teeth loses her husband, and a woman with gaps between her teeth becomes unchaste <term>Kulata: a woman who strays from her family or is of loose character</term>. ||100||

---

## Now, the characteristics of the tongue are described

> A tongue that is red, soft, and white <note>likely referring to a clean, healthy surface</note> makes one a consumer of desired things. 
> One that is narrow in the middle and wide at the front leads to sorrow. ||101||

<header>The Nativity of Women</header>

With a white tongue, death by water; with a dark one, she loves quarrels.
With a fleshy tongue, she is poor; with a long one, she eats forbidden things. ||102||
With a broad tongue, a woman is prone to great intoxication and folly.

Meaning—A woman whose tongue is reddish and soft enjoys desirable and sweet foods. Likewise, a woman with a white-colored tongue that is wide at the front and narrow in the middle suffers misery. ||101|| <note>The Sanskrit verse 101 is partially implied in the prose commentary provided in the source.</note> And a woman with a white tongue dies from the distress of water <note>likely meaning drowning or water-borne disease</note>, and a woman with a black tongue loves to fight. A woman with a thick, fleshy tongue remains poor, and a woman with a long tongue eats things that are not fit to be eaten. ||102|| And a woman with a very wide tongue is a great liar and is full of intoxication.

### Now, the Characteristics of the Palate

Glossy, resembling a red lotus, and consistently soft is the palate. ||103||
With a white palate, widowhood; with a yellow one, she becomes an ascetic.
With a black one, she is pained by the loss of children; with a rough one, she has a large family. ||104||

Meaning—If a woman’s palate is glossy, smooth, and has the redness of a lotus flower, then that woman with a soft palate is considered excellent. ||103|| And if a woman has a white palate, she becomes a widow; one with a yellow palate becomes a female ascetic <note>original: "pravrajita," one who renounces worldly life</note>. A woman with a black palate is distressed by the separation from her offspring, and one with a dry or rough palate has a very large family to support. ||104||

### Now, the Characteristics of the Uvula

In the throat, thick, well-rounded, gradually tapering, and very red.
A uvula that does not hang too low is auspicious; one that is thick and black brings sorrow. ||105||

<note>The vocabulary section defines technical terms used: "rasanaya" (tongue), "talulakshana" (traits of the palate), "ghantikalakshana" (traits of the uvula), "vaidhavyam" (widowhood), "pravrajita" (female ascetic), "daridrini" (poor woman), "kokanadabhasam" (resembling a red lotus), and "sulohita" (very red/blood-colored).</note>

<summary>This page details the divinatory significance of a woman's tongue, palate, and uvula, linking physical colors and shapes to her future character, wealth, and family life. It reflects ancient Indian physiognomy (Samudrika Shastra) where physical traits were believed to reveal destiny.</summary>

# With Hindi Commentary
Page 31

**Meaning**—A woman whose uvula <note>the fleshy lobe hanging at the back of the throat</note> is thick, round, and possesses a bright reddish hue is considered auspicious. Conversely, if it is long, thick, and black, it is considered inauspicious. ||105||

### Now, the Characteristics of the Smile

> A smile where the teeth are barely visible, the cheeks are slightly raised,
> and the eyes do not close, is praised in beautiful-eyed women. ||106||

**Meaning**—A woman whose teeth are only slightly visible when she laughs, whose cheeks rise slightly, and whose eyes do not shut tight possesses an excellent and auspicious laugh. ||106||

### Now, the Characteristics of the Nose

> A nose with even, rounded nostrils and small openings brings good fortune.
> One with a thick tip, depressed in the middle, or overly prominent is not praised. ||107||
> A nose that is contracted or red at the tip brings the suffering of widowhood.
> A flat nose indicates a servant, while a very short or very long nose indicates a love for quarreling. ||108||

**Meaning**—A woman whose nose has even, rounded nostrils and small openings is auspicious. A nose that is thick at the tip, sunken in the middle, and high at the bridge is not considered favorable. ||107|| A woman whose nose is narrow or red at the tip suffers the pain of widowhood. One with a flat nose becomes a messenger or servant <note>original: "parapreshya" - one sent on errands for others</note>, and a woman with an exceptionally short or long nose is fond of fighting. ||108||

### Now, the Characteristics of the Sneeze

> A long sneeze, or two or three sneezes in quick succession, grants long life.

**Meaning**—A woman who, after a deep breath, sneezes two or three times in a row, attains a very long lifespan.

<vocab><term>Samudrika Shastra</term> (the study of body marks), <term>Lakshana</term> (auspicious traits), <term>Nasika</term> (nose), <term>Hasana</term> (laughter/smiling), <term>Chhik</term> (sneezing), <term>Sudrisham</term> (beautiful-eyed women), <term>Kapolakam</term> (cheeks), <term>Vaidhavya</term> (widowhood)</vocab>

<header>Female Horoscopy</header>

# Now, the characteristics of the eyes are described

A woman's eyes are considered auspicious if the corners are reddish and the pupils are black. ||109||
Eyes that are clear and white like cow's milk, large, glossy, and framed by black eyelashes are favorable. A woman with protruding eyes does not live a long life, while one with perfectly round eyes becomes unchaste <note>original: "kulata" - a woman who strays from her family or is of loose character</note>. ||110||
Eyes resembling those of a ram, a buffalo, or eyes that are squinted <note>original: "kekara" - squint-eyed or cross-eyed</note> are not auspicious. A woman with tawny eyes like a cow's is constantly overcome by lust and is of very bad conduct. ||111||
One with eyes like a pigeon is of ill-disposition; red eyes indicate one who might harm her husband; sunken eyes <note>original: "kotara" - hollow or deep-set like a tree cavity</note> indicate a wicked nature; and elephant-like eyes are not auspicious. ||112||
A woman blind in the left eye is a harlot <note>original: "pumshchali" - one who runs after men</note>, while one blind in the right eye is barren. A woman whose eyes are honey-colored <note>original: "madhu-pingakshi" - yellowish-brown like honey</note> is blessed with an abundance of wealth and grain. ||113||

**Meaning—** The eyes of a woman that possess a reddish tint at the corners and black pupils are auspicious ||109||. Those that have the whiteness of cow's milk, are large, smooth, and have black pupils are auspicious. A woman with eyes that are not raised or protruding attains a long life, whereas one with round eyes is unchaste ||110||. Eyes like those of a ram, a buffalo, or squinted eyes are not good. Eyes that are tawny-colored like a cow's indicate a woman who is always ready for the arts of love <note>referring to "kamakala" or erotic desires</note> ||111||. Furthermore, pigeon-like eyes indicate a bad temperament; red eyes indicate a destroyer of the husband; and sunken eyes indicate a wicked woman. Elephant-like eyes are not auspicious ||112||. A woman who is one-eyed in the left eye is a prostitute, and one who is one-eyed in the right eye is sterile. A woman with eyes yellow like honey is endowed with wealth, grain, and various prosperities ||113||.

# Now, the characteristics of the eyelashes are described

A woman possessing eyelashes that are very thick, glossy, black, and fine is endowed with great good fortune.
If the eyelashes are brownish-red, sparse, or coarse, the woman is considered blameworthy or ill-fated. ||114||

<summary>This page details the physiognomic interpretation of a woman's eyes and eyelashes, correlating physical traits like color and shape with moral character, longevity, and future prosperity.</summary>
<keywords>physiognomy, female horoscopy, eye characteristics, eyelashes, divination, character traits, Stri-Jatakam</keywords>

<header>With Hindi Commentary</header>

Meaning—A woman whose eyelashes are thick, glossy, dark-tinted, and fine brings good fortune. Conversely, a woman who possesses reddish-brown <note>original: "kapila-varna"</note>, sparse, and coarse eyelashes is considered unlucky. 114.

### Now, the Characteristics of Eyebrows

> Two well-rounded, thin, glossy, black, and unjoined;
> Praiseworthy are those with soft hair, the beautiful-browed, shaped like a bow. 115.
> Coarse-haired, thick, scattered, and straight in a woman;
> Unfavorable are those joined together, long-haired, or reddish-yellow. 116.

Meaning—A woman whose eyebrows are glossy, black, and not joined to one another, possessing a bow-like curvature <note>original: "karmuka-akriti" - shaped like a traditional archer's bow</note>, is auspicious. Those with soft hair and a crescent shape are favorable. 115. Furthermore, eyebrows with harsh hair (resembling the hair of a donkey), or those that are thick, spread out, straight, joined in the middle, very long, or of a reddish-yellow <term>Pingala</term> <note>a tawny or brownish-yellow color often associated with fiery or unstable temperaments in traditional texts</note> hue are not auspicious. 116.

### Now, the Characteristics of Ears

> Long ears, well-rounded, are givers of happiness and auspiciousness;
> Those lacking a visible outer rim, veiny, crooked, or thin, are condemned. 117.

Meaning—A woman whose ears are long and rounded is a bringer of happiness; such ears are auspicious. Those that lack width <note>referring to the "shashkuli" or the outer ear's curved rim</note>, are heavily veined, weak, or crooked are inauspicious. 117.

### Now, the Characteristics of the Forehead

> A forehead free of veins, hairless, and resembling the half-moon;
> Not sunken and measuring three fingers in width, is the cause of good fortune and health for a woman. 118.
> A forehead clearly marked with the sign of the Swastika leads to royal wealth;
> One whose forehead is excessively protruding surely brings ruin to her husband's younger brother. 119.

<note>The "three fingers" measurement is a traditional Ayurvedic and physiognomic unit of proportion.</note>

->A woman with a hairy, veined, or overly high forehead is considered prone to illness. ||120||<-

**Meaning**—A woman whose forehead is free of visible veins and hair, shaped like a half-moon, and measures three fingers in height is considered fortunate and healthy. ||118|| One who possesses a radiant and auspicious line across the forehead should be known as a bringer of royal wealth. ### Now, the Characteristics of the Parting of the Hair

->A straight parting of the hair is praised—<-

**Meaning**—A woman whose hair-parting is perfectly straight is considered auspicious.

### Now, the Characteristics of the Head

->One shaped like an elephant's temple and rounded indicates good fortune and sovereignty. ||121||<-

**Meaning**—She whose head is high, rounded like the frontal lobes of an elephant <note>original: "Gajakumbha," a traditional symbol of majesty and strength</note>, is a bringer of good fortune and prosperity. ||121||

### Now, the Characteristics of the Crown of the Head

->A thick crown indicates widowhood; a long head indicates a captive or unchaste woman.<-
->Even with a broad head, one becomes a vessel of misfortune. ||122||<-

**Meaning**—A woman whose crown <note>the "choti" or top-most point of the head</note> is overly thick or fleshy is destined for widowhood. If the crown area is excessively large, she is considered sinful, and a woman with an oversized head is a recipient of ill-fate. ||122||

### Now, the Characteristics of the Hair

->Hair with the luster of a swarm of bees, fine, glossy, and very soft,<-
->With slightly curled tips and a wavy texture, is exceedingly beautiful. ||123||<-

<note>The "luster of a swarm of bees" refers to a deep, iridescent black color highly prized in classical Indian aesthetics.</note>

<note>original: "Parushah kutilagrashcha..."</note>
Coarse hair, with crooked ends, sparse, 
Reddish-brown, light, and dry—
These lead to sorrow, poverty, and bondage. ||124||

**Meaning**—The hair of a woman whose locks are curly, fine, smooth, soft, and coiled like earrings at the ends, and deep black in color, is considered extremely auspicious. ||123|| <note>Verse 123 is summarized here in the commentary despite the Sanskrit verse appearing on the previous page.</note> Conversely, hair that is crooked at the ends, thin, reddish-brown <note>original: "pingal-varna," often implying a tawny or brassy discoloration</note>, short, and brittle brings about suffering, poverty, and restriction. ||124||

Therefore, a wise man, having examined a maiden 
Possessing the auspicious marks, 
Should marry her, so that he may never 
Become a vessel for misfortune. ||125||

Thus ends the second chapter, titled "The Description of the Sixty-Six Marks," in the *Strijataka* composed by the Royal Astrologer, Pandit Shyamlal, son of Shri Baldev Prasad, the jewel of the Gauda lineage residing in Vanshavareli. ||2||

**Meaning**—First, a wise man should examine a maiden for the excellent qualities mentioned in the preceding signs and then marry her; by doing so, he does not encounter distress through marriage. ||125||

Thus ends the second chapter, titled "The Description of the Sixty-Six Marks," in the *Shyamsundari* Hindi Commentary written by the Royal Astrologer, Pandit Shyamlal, son of Shri Baldev Prasad, the jewel of the Gauda lineage residing in Vanshavareli. ||2||

### Now, I shall duly explain the signs of moles, warts, and marks,
### By the mere knowledge of which, one does not sink into the ocean of sorrow. ||1||

**Meaning**—Now Lord Skanda <note>The deity Kartikeya, often the narrator of these esoteric sciences</note> says: O Agastya! I shall now describe the characteristics of moles <term>Til: small dark spots on the skin</term>, warts <term>Mashaka: raised skin growths</term>, and birthmarks <term>Lahasana: port-wine stains or large marks</term>, etc. By simply knowing these, a man does not drown in the ocean of misery. ||1||

->Now, the signs of moles and warts between the eyebrows are described<-

A wart or mark located between the eyebrows or on the forehead indicates royal status.

### Now, the mark of a mole on the tip of the nose

<header>The Science of Female Destiny</header>

## Now, regarding the Trident mark on the forehead

->If the Creator <note>original: "Svayambhuva" (the self-born Brahma)</note> has fashioned a trident mark upon the forehead,<-
->that woman shall attain sovereignty over thousands of other women. ||9||<-

**Meaning—**A woman upon whose forehead the mark of a trident <note>The *Trishula*, a three-pronged sacred symbol associated with Shiva</note> has been made by Brahma, becomes the leader or mistress of a thousand women. ||9||

## Now, regarding the trait of grinding teeth

->She who, while sleeping, makes a 'kiti-kiti' sound by grinding her teeth together,<-
->even if she possesses other good qualities, is considered inauspicious; as is she who talks in her sleep. ||10||<-

**Meaning—**A woman who grinds or gnashes her teeth together in the midst of sleep, or talks nonsense while sleeping, is considered unfavorable <note>original: "neshta" (undesirable/unlucky)</note> even if she is otherwise endowed with auspicious physical features. ||10||

## Now, regarding the characteristics of hair whorls

->A clockwise whorl on the hand is blessed; a counter-clockwise one is not beautiful.<-

**Meaning—**A woman who has a clockwise whorl <note>term: "Dakshinavarta" (turning to the right/clockwise)</note> on her hands, or specifically a clockwise whorl of hair on the back of her hands, finds it to be auspicious. A counter-clockwise whorl <note>term: "Vamavarta" (turning to the left/counter-clockwise)</note> is inauspicious.

## Now, regarding the whorl on the navel

->A clockwise whorl on the navel, the ear, or the chest is praised. ||11||<-

**Meaning—**A woman who has a clockwise whorl of hair on her navel, her ear, or her heart/chest area finds it to be auspicious. A counter-clockwise whorl in these places is inauspicious. ||11||

## Now, regarding the whorl on the back

->A clockwise whorl on the right side of the spine leads to happiness.<-

**Meaning—**A woman who has a clockwise whorl of hair on the right side of her back finds it to be a sign of good fortune and happiness.

### Now, regarding the circular mark on the back

**If there is a mark on the back, centered and resembling a navel, it grants long life and the increase of sons. ||12||**
Meaning—A woman who has a circular mark <note>original: "chakra"</note> in the center of her back, similar to a navel, gives birth to sons who live long lives and she sees her progeny flourish. ||12||

### Now, regarding the mark on the pubic forehead

**A clockwise mark on the crown of the vulva is seen on a queen.**
**If it resembles a broken cart, it bestows the happiness of many sons. ||13||**
Meaning—A woman who possesses a clockwise-turning <note>original: "dakshinavarta"</note> circular mark on the upper part of the pubic area becomes the wife of a king. If the mark on that area resembles the shape of a broken cart <note>original: "shakata-bhanga"</note>, that woman enjoys the happiness of having many sons. ||13||

### Now, regarding the mark on the waist and private parts

**A whorl located on the waist or the private area destroys the husband and offspring.**
Meaning—A woman who has a whorl of hair <note>original: "romavali chakra"</note> on her waist or within the private regions causes the destruction of her husband and her children.

### Now, regarding the mark on the back and abdomen

**Whorls on the abdomen and the back are not auspicious. ||14||**
**With one, she destroys her husband; with another, she becomes unchaste.**
Meaning—A woman who has hair whorls on both her stomach and her back is considered inauspicious. ||14|| If there is only one such mark, she brings about the end of her master <note>husband</note>, and if both marks are present, that woman becomes a wanderer or unchaste <term>punshchali: a woman who pursues other men</term>.

### Now, the characteristics of the mark on the throat

**A clockwise whorl on the throat is the cause of sorrow and widowhood. ||15||**

-># Female Horoscopy<-
<note>original: "Stri-Jatakam" — a branch of astrology and physiognomy focusing on the lives and characteristics of women.</note>

Meaning—And if there is a whorl of hair <term>Romavali</term><note>A line or pattern of fine body hair.</note> on the throat, that woman suffers from many types of diseases and becomes a widow. ||15||

### Now, regarding the whorl on the parting of the hair and the forehead

Whether on the parting of the hair, or on the forehead, or in the middle of the nape of the neck, such a woman should be avoided from a distance with great effort. She destroys her husband within a year. ||16||
Meaning—The woman who has a hair whorl in the parting of her hair, on her forehead, or on the nape of her neck <note>original: "Krikatikam" — the joint of the neck or nape.</note> destroys her husband within just one year. One should abandon her from a distance. ||16||

### Now, regarding the whorl on the crown of the head

Whether clockwise or counter-clockwise, a whorl of hair on a woman; whether there is one on the head or two, even if it is on the left and moving to the left. ||17||
Within ten days, those two destroy the husband; the wise man should abandon her from a distance.
Meaning—If a woman has one or two hair whorls at the place of the crest <note>original: "Shikha-sthane" — the crown where the traditional hair tuft is kept.</note>, whether they turn clockwise <term>Dakshinavarta</term> or counter-clockwise <term>Vamavarta</term>, the counter-clockwise whorl is inauspicious <term>Neshta</term>. ||17|| Such a woman destroys her husband within ten days; a wise man should avoid her from a distance.

### Now, regarding the whorl on the waist

One with a whorl on the waist is unchaste—
Meaning—She who has a counter-clockwise hair whorl on her waist becomes an unchaste woman <term>Kulata</term>.

### Now, the characteristics of the whorl on the navel

—One with a whorl on the navel is a devoted wife. ||18||
Meaning—She who has a whorl in her navel <note>original: "Tundi" — colloquial for navel/belly button.</note> is a faithful and devoted wife <term>Pativrata</term>. ||18||

->Now, regarding the whorl on the back<-

A woman with a whorl on her back becomes a destroyer of her husband or a woman of loose character. ||19||

->Skanda spoke<-

->Now, regarding the rejection of a woman despite good physical marks<-

Even if she possesses good marks, if she is of bad character, she is the crown jewel of ill-omened women.
**Meaning—** Now Lord Skanda <note>The deity Kartikeya, often the narrator of such wisdom</note> says that a woman who is endowed with all auspicious physical marks but is of bad character and an adulteress should be abandoned in every way. Even if she lacks auspicious marks, if she is virtuous, she is considered as having all good marks. ||20||
**Meaning—** A woman who is possessed of all physical ill-omens but is a faithful wife <note>original: "pativratā"</note> is counted as the leader among women with all auspicious marks. ||20||

->Now, the yoga for obtaining an excellent wife<-

A woman of good marks, good character, obedient, and who treats her husband as a god, is obtained in the home only through the grace of the Lord of the Universe. ||21||
**Meaning—** A woman with all auspicious marks, endowed with good character, who is under the authority of her husband and for whom the husband is a deity—such a woman is obtained in the home only by the grace of Lord Shiva <note>original: "Vishvesha"</note>. ||21||

->Now, the cause of beauty in women<-

Those by whom well-dressed women were adorned with various kinds of ornaments in a previous birth, indeed become beautiful themselves. ||22||

<meta>This page continues the discourse on the karmic origins of a woman's physical and social fortune, transitioning from the causes of beauty to the spiritual merits that grant a devoted husband and long life.</meta>

<page-num>42</page-num>
<header>The Nativity of Women <note>original: "Stri-Jatakam"; a branch of astrology focusing specifically on the horoscopes and destinies of women</note></header>

Those women by whom the body was cleansed in sacred waters, or who departed their lives therein;
They become rivers of beauty in this world, endowed with auspicious signs. ||23||

**Meaning—**The woman who, in a previous birth, adorned young virgins or the wives of Brahmins <note>the priestly class</note> with various garments and ornaments, becomes beautiful and well-formed in this birth. ||22|| That woman who, in a previous birth, bathed her body in holy pilgrimage sites <note>original: "tirthas"</note> or gave up her life in such sacred places, becomes a woman of superior beauty, endowed with every auspicious physical mark. ||23||

### Now, regarding the devotion of the husband

Those by whom the Mother of the Worlds, the consort of Shiva <note>original: "Mrida-vadhu," referring to Parvati or Durga</note>, was worshipped here;
Those women become of excellent character and possess husbands who are devoted to them. ||24||
For those deer-eyed women of good conduct whose husbands are under their influence;
The fruits of their auspicious signs are indeed like heaven and liberation found right here. ||25||

**Meaning—**Those women who have worshipped Parvati or Durga Bhavani <note>the Divine Mother</note> in this life become endowed with all virtues, possess excellent character, and keep their husbands devoted to them. ||24|| And for those women whose husbands are devoted to them and who possess a beautiful nature, both heaven and liberation <note>original: "moksha"; release from the cycle of rebirth</note> are found right here in this world. One should certainly know this to be the fruit for women with such excellent signs. ||25||

### Now, following the topic of the virtuous wife, regarding long life

By their auspicious signs and even by their good conduct,
Women make even a short-lived husband long-lived, for they are the abode of joy. ||26||

<vocab>The Nativity of Women, Possessing auspicious signs, Devotion of the husband, Consort of Shiva (Parvati), One whose husband is devoted to her, Deer-eyed women, Heaven and Liberation, A virtuous/chaste woman, Longevity, Abode of joy</vocab>

<summary>This page explains that beauty and a devoted husband are rewards for past-life charity and worship of the Goddess Parvati. It emphasizes that a virtuous wife's spiritual merit can even extend the lifespan of her husband.</summary>

->*Those learned in the rules of horoscopy <term>Horā: the study of natal astrology and time</term> say that the birth charts of men and women are similar;<-
->however, any results predicted for women that seem inappropriate for them should instead be applied to their husbands.*<-

---

> *Varaha <note>Referring to Varahamihira, the 6th-century Indian astronomer and polymath</note>:— "Whatever results are possible for women in a human birth, all those should be declared; however, if a result is unsuitable for her, it should be applied entirely to her husband. Her widowhood is seen from the eighth house, her physical body from the Ascendant and the Moon, and her husband's nature from the seventh house. ||1||"*

And likewise— "The physical body should be considered from the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</term> and the Moon; the husband's wealth and status from the seventh house; childbirth from the fifth house; and her widowhood from the eighth house. ||2|| Whatever results have been stated by experts in horoscopy for the birth-time of men, these same should be applied to women. If a result is truly unsuitable for her, it must be understood as applying to her leader <note>original: "Nayaka," meaning husband or protector</note>. ||3||" And in another text— "The planetary periods <term>Dasha: time cycles in Vedic astrology</term> are the same for both men and women as previously stated. Whatever is unsuitable for her, such as the husband's fortune, all that should be declared as belonging to her lord." ||4||

# Female Horoscopy
<note>original: "Stri-jātakam" — a branch of astrology specifically concerning the horoscopes of women.</note>

**From the seventh house, one should speak of the husband; if a malefic planet is in the eighth, the husband's death occurs. From the rising sign of the Moon, her own body and physical condition should be considered. ||1||**

Meaning—The results that experts in the science of astrology <note>original: "Jyotish-shastra"</note> have described for the birth of men should also be applied to women. For instance, the consideration of the maximum lifespan and planetary periods <note>original: "paramayu-dasha"</note> should be stated equally for both. However, those results which are not applicable to women—such as royal combinations <note>original: "Rajayoga"</note> or other factors indicating power—should instead be interpreted as bringing fortune to the woman's husband. One should judge the auspiciousness of the husband from the seventh house from the Ascendant or the Moon. The death of the husband is to be considered from the eighth house <note>original: "ashtam-sthan"</note> from the Ascendant or the Moon. Finally, the physical health and body of the woman herself should be judged from the place where the Ascendant and the Moon are situated. ||1||

### Now, the places for considering widowhood, marital fortune, happiness, and beauty are stated:

**Widowhood is seen in the house of death (8th); marital fortune and happiness in the seventh. Beauty should be considered in the house of the Ascendant (1st), and the wealth of sons in the ninth. ||2||**

**In these houses, gentle planets <note>benefics</note> are known to give auspicious results when endowed with strength. Cruel planets <note>malefics</note>, however, do not give desired results; this should always be considered in relation to the lords of those houses. ||3||**

Meaning—A woman's potential for widowhood <note>original: "vaidhavya-yoga"</note> is to be judged from the eighth house. The fortune and happiness derived from the husband should be judged from the seventh house. The beauty of the body is to be seen from the Ascendant <note>the 1st house</note>, and the prosperity of sons is to be judged from the ninth house. ||2|| In the birth charts of women, if auspicious planets <note>original: "shubh graha"</note> are seated in the aforementioned houses, they grant good results. If "sinful" or cruel planets <note>original: "paap-graha" or malefics</note> are situated in these places, they give undesirable results. This is especially true if the lords of these aforementioned houses...

If malefic planets are situated in their own signs, they should not be called unfavorable; rather, they will be givers of excellent results. || 3 ||

### Regarding the Yoga of Masculine Appearance

> If the Ascendant and the Moon are in masculine signs and masculine divisions, and are joined with malefic planets, the girl born is of masculine form and character, unsuitable for a husband, and ill-behaved. || 4 ||

**Meaning**—In the birth chart of a woman, if the <term>Ascendant</term> <note>the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note> and the <term>Moon</term> are situated in masculine signs—namely Aries, Gemini, Leo, Libra, Sagittarius, or Aquarius—and are also situated in the <term>Navamsha</term> <note>the ninth divisional chart used to determine the hidden strength of planets</note> of these same signs, and if the <term>Ascendant and Moon</term> are joined with or <term>aspected</term> <note>influenced by the "glance" or mathematical alignment of another planet</note> by <term>malefic planets</term> <note>planets like Mars or Saturn that typically bring challenges</note>, then that girl possesses a masculine nature, is not suitable for a husband, and is of poor character. || 4 || And so says **Gunakar:** <note>original Sanskrit: "pundehashilashahita..."</note> "If both [Ascendant and Moon] are in masculine signs, joined or aspected by cruel planets, she possesses a male body and character."

---

### Regarding the Yoga of Feminine Appearance

> If the Ascendant and the Moon are in even signs and even divisions, the girl is endowed with feminine qualities. If joined or aspected by benefics, she is fortunate, virtuous, and well-renowned. || 5 ||

**Meaning**—In the birth chart of a woman, if both the <term>Ascendant and the Moon</term> are situated in <term>even signs</term> <note>feminine signs</note>—namely Taurus, Cancer, Virgo, Scorpio, Capricorn, or Pisces—and in the <term>Navamsha</term> of these same signs, then that woman possesses a feminine appearance and is endowed with feminine virtues. If those same <term>Ascendant and Moon situated in even signs</term> are aspected by or joined with <term>benefic planets</term> <note>auspicious planets like Jupiter or Venus</note>, then that woman becomes famously fortunate and a devoted wife in the world. || 5 || **Varaha:** <note>original Sanskrit: "yugmeshulagnashashinoh..." referring to Varahamihira, the 6th-century astronomer</note> "If the Ascendant and Moon are in even signs, the woman is established in her natural state, endowed with good character and ornaments, provided they are aspected by benefics. If in odd signs, she has a masculine appearance and nature; if also aspected by malefics, she is sinful and devoid of virtues." || 1 || **Gunakar:** <note>original Sanskrit: "chandrangayoh samagrihe..."</note> "If the Moon and the body [Ascendant] are in even houses, the woman is established in her nature and endowed with beauty; if aspected by benefics, she is of good character."

### On the Combination of Mixed Characteristics

> If the Ascendant <note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note> and the Moon are situated in odd signs and odd Navamshas <note>A ninth-part divisional chart used for precision</note>, and are joined or aspected by benefic planets, the woman born will possess mixed qualities, a balanced physical form, steady gait, and great wisdom. However, if these two are in even signs and are joined or aspected by malefic planets, the woman will likewise have a mixed nature and temperament. Furthermore, one should determine the woman's entire lifespan by observing the strength of the planets that aspect or are joined with the lords of the signs occupied by the Ascendant and the Moon. ||6||

**Meaning—** In the birth chart of a woman, if the Moon is placed in an odd sign and an odd ninth-division (Navamsha) and is associated with or viewed by auspicious planets, that woman will have moderate virtues, a balanced gait, and be intelligent. If the Ascendant and Moon are in even signs and even ninth-divisions and are associated with or viewed by inauspicious planets, she will have a mixed temperament and mixed virtues. Alternatively, by observing the strength of the planets that aspect the lords of the Ascendant and Moon signs, the total longevity of the woman should be predicted. ||6||

### On the Consideration of the Strength of the Trimsamsha

> Between the Ascendant and the Moon, whichever is stronger, the results should be declared based on its Trimsamsha <note>A divisional chart where a sign is divided into 30 degrees, but assigned to five planets (excluding the Sun and Moon) in specific portions</note>. The results declared for the Trimsamsha naturally come to pass based on its strength. ||7||

**Meaning—** In the birth charts of women, one should state the results based on the Trimsamsha of whichever is more powerful: the Ascendant or the Moon. I shall now describe the natural results based on the strength of the Trimsamsha. ||7||

### Results According to the Trimsamsha

### Results of the Trimsamsha of the Ascendant or Moon in the House of Mars

> If the Ascendant or the Moon falls in a sign of Mars, the results for the Trimsamshas of Mars and the others in order are: a wicked girl, a very crafty woman, a virtuous woman, one of bad conduct, and a servant. ||8||

**Meaning—** For a woman whose Ascendant or Moon is in the signs of Mars, namely Aries or Scorpio...

If the Ascendant or the Moon is situated in a sign of Mars <note>Aries or Scorpio</note> and falls within the Trimsamsha of Mars, that woman will enjoy the company of another man before her marriage. If that same Ascendant or Moon is in the Trimsamsha of Mercury, she will be deceitful and a creator of illusions. If it is in the Trimsamsha of Jupiter, she will be a devoted and faithful wife. If it is in the Trimsamsha of Venus, she will follow a wicked livelihood and be highly censurable. And if that same Ascendant or Moon in the 1st or 8th sign <note>Aries or Scorpio</note> is in the Trimsamsha of Saturn, she will be a servant. ||8||

And so says Varaha <note>Varahamihira, the 6th-century astronomer/astrologer</note>: "**In the signs of the son of the Earth <note>Mars</note>, according to the Trimsamshas of Mars, Saturn, Jupiter, Mercury, and Venus respectively, a girl becomes wicked, a servant, virtuous, deceitful, or of bad character.** ||1||" And also: "**When the stronger of the Ascendant or Moon is in the house of Mars, in the Trimsamsha of Venus, the girl is highly censurable; in Jupiter's, she is exceedingly virtuous; in Mars', she is said to be wicked; in Mercury's, she is deceptive; and in the Trimsamsha of the son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note>, she becomes a servant—these are the results in order.**" And also in the *Hora Ratna*: "**In the sign of Mars and the Trimsamsha of Mars, a girl is devoid of virtues and her children may die; in the Trimsamsha of Saturn, she is a servant and possesses various bad habits. ||1|| In the Trimsamsha of Jupiter, she is blessed with sons, though distressed by heavy expenses, and is a devoted wife; in the Trimsamsha of Mercury, she is very deceptive, of impure conduct, and has few children. ||2|| In the Trimsamsha of Venus, she is the mother of daughters and satisfied by the enjoyment of a paramour. The results for the Trimsamshas of the Sun should be declared similarly. ||3||**"

### Now, the results according to the Trimsamshas in the house of Mercury

->In the house of the son of the Lord of Stars <note>Mercury's signs: Gemini or Virgo</note>, if in the Trimsamsha of the son of the Earth <note>Mars</note>, she is a hypocrite; in that of Venus, she is of low desire; in that of the son of the Moon <note>Mercury</note>, she is highly endowed with virtues; in that of the Priest of the Lord of Gods <note>Jupiter</note>, she is indeed exceedingly faithful; and in the Trimsamsha of the son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note>, she is declared by the wise to be the wife of an impotent man. ||9||<-

# Female Horoscopy

Meaning—If at the time of a woman's birth, the Ascendant <note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</note> and the Moon are situated in the signs of Mercury (Gemini or Virgo) and fall within the *Trimsamsha* <note>A specific 1-degree division used in Vedic astrology to determine character</note> of Mars, then that girl will be deceitful. If the same Ascendant and Moon are in the *Trimsamsha* of Venus, she will have little interest in sexual pleasure. If they are in the *Trimsamsha* of Mercury, she will be endowed with many virtues. If they are in the *Trimsamsha* of Jupiter, she will be a devoted and chaste wife. If they are in the *Trimsamsha* of Saturn, she will be the wife of a eunuch or an impotent man. ||9||

It is also stated in the *Horamakaranda* <note>original: "स्यात्कापटी गुणयुताथ सती बुधर्क्षे विक्षिप्तमन्मथमयो च नपुंसकश्च।"</note>: "In Mercury's sign, she may be deceitful, virtuous, chaste, distracted by desire, or joined to a eunuch [depending on the division]." So too says Varaha <note>The famous astronomer Varahamihira</note>: "In Mercury's house, she is deceitful, like a eunuch, chaste, rich in virtues, or of scattered desires." *Vriddhayavana* <note>An ancient authority on "Yavana" or Greek-influenced Indian astrology</note> says: "In Mercury's house and Mars's division, the girl loves paramours and has few sons. ||1|| In Saturn's division, she is like a eunuch, her children may die, or she joins with another's husband. She is virtuous and dear to her husband if in Jupiter's division, especially if Jupiter is exalted. In Mercury's own division, she is of a noble family, and in Venus's division, she possesses cattle, wealth, and enjoyments." ||2||

### Now, the results according to the Trimsamsha in Jupiter's House

->If the Moon or the Ascendant is in the house of the Teacher of the Gods <note>Jupiter; signs Sagittarius or Pisces</note> in the division of Mars,<-
->she is virtuous; in the division of the Teacher of the Gods, she is famous for a multitude of qualities.<-
->In the division of the Son of the Moon <note>Mercury</note>, she has beautiful wealth; in the division of Venus, she is surely chaste;<-
->and in the division of the Son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note>, the wise say she has little interest in sexual pleasure. ||10||<-

Meaning—If at the time of a girl's birth, the Ascendant or the Moon is situated in the signs of Jupiter (9/12, Sagittarius or Pisces) and falls within the *Trimsamsha* of Mars, then that woman is virtuous among women. If the same Ascendant and Moon are in the *Trimsamsha* of Jupiter, she is famous for her many good qualities. If the same Ascendant and Moon are in the *Trimsamsha* of Mercury, then that girl possesses excellent wealth...

...and if the Ascendant or the Moon is situated in the <term>Trimsamsa</term><note>A 1/30th division of a zodiac sign used in Vedic astrology to determine specific character traits</note> of Venus, then that woman is devoted to her husband. And so says **Varahamihira** <note>A famous 6th-century Indian astronomer and astrologer</note>:
"In a sign of Jupiter, if the Trimsamsa belongs to Mars, the maiden is well-known as unchaste; in the portion of Saturn, she is poor, a mother of only daughters, and devoted to independence. ||1|| In the portion of Jupiter, she is very wealthy; in the portion of Mercury, the woman is honored by the people. In the portion of Venus, she possesses the six virtues, is devoted to her husband, and is virtuous. ||2||"

### Now, the results according to the Trimsamsa in the House of Venus

->In the house of the teacher of the demons <note>original: "Daityacharya" - referring to the planet Venus</note>, if the Ascendant or the Lord of the Stars <note>original: "Udunayaka" - the Moon</note> is in the portion of the minister of the King of Gods <note>original: "Surendrasachiva" - the planet Jupiter</note>, she is virtuous; in the portion of Mars, she is exceedingly wicked. In the portion of Mercury, she is highly skilled in various arts; in the portion of Venus, she is endowed with shining and expert qualities; and in the portion of the son of the Sun <note>original: "Divamanisuta" - the planet Saturn</note>, she becomes a remarried woman <note>original: "Punarbhu" - a woman who marries a second time</note>. ||11||<-

**Meaning—** For a maiden at whose time of birth the <term>Lagna</term><note>The Ascendant or the sign rising on the eastern horizon</note> and the Moon are situated in the signs of Venus (Taurus or Libra) and placed in the Trimsamsa of Jupiter, that maiden is virtuous among women. If that same Ascendant or Moon is situated in the Trimsamsa of Mars, then that maiden is wicked. If situated in the Trimsamsa of Mercury, then that maiden is skilled in all clever arts. If that same Ascendant or Moon is situated in the Trimsamsa of Venus, then that maiden is endowed with radiant qualities. And if that same Ascendant or Moon is situated in the Trimsamsa of Saturn, then that woman has two marriages. ||11||

Furthermore, from the **Horamakaranda**:
"In the house of Venus, she is wicked, skilled in arts, virtuous, and famous for her qualities, or a remarried woman [depending on the portion]."

And another from **Vriddhayavana** <note>An ancient astrological text often attributed to "the Elder Greek/Ionian"</note>:
"In the house of Venus, in the portion of Mars, she is wicked..."

# Female Horoscopy

...she is fond of wicked people and hates her husband. If in the subdivision of Saturn <note>original: "Mandanshe"</note>, she becomes a remarried widow, her children die, and she is constantly afflicted by diseases. ||1||
If in the subdivision of Jupiter <note>original: "Jivanshe"</note>, she is endowed with beauty and virtues, and blessed with a husband and sons. If in the subdivision of Mercury <note>original: "Saumyanshe"</note>, she is of bad character, yet satisfied by the arts of poetry and song. ||2||
If in the subdivision of Venus <note>original: "Shukranshe"</note>, she is a woman of pleasure, the beloved of a clever man, and famous throughout the world. If the planet is joined with a malefic or is weak, the results of these thirty-degree subdivisions <term>Trishamsha: a 1/30th division of a zodiac sign used to determine specific character traits</term> will manifest fully. ||3||

### Now, the results according to the subdivisions within the House of Saturn

-> In the house of Saturn, in the degrees of Mars, she is a servant; in those of Mercury, <-
-> the girl is wicked. In those of Jupiter, she is devoted to her husband; in those of Venus, <-
-> she is barren; and in those of the son of the Sun (Saturn) himself, she is lowly. ||12|| <-

**Meaning—** If at the time of a woman's birth, the Ascendant or the Moon is situated in the 10th or 11th signs (Capricorn or Aquarius, the houses of Saturn) and falls within the subdivision of **Mars**, that girl becomes a servant. If that same Ascendant or Moon is in the subdivision of **Mercury**, the woman is wicked. If it is in the subdivision of **Jupiter**, the woman is devoted to her husband as if he were a god (a faithful wife). If it is in the subdivision of **Venus**, the woman is barren. And if the Ascendant or Moon is in the subdivision of **Saturn** itself, the woman associates with low-born or base men. ||12|| 

**Gunakara states:** "A servant, a wicked woman, a devotee of her husband, a barren woman, and one attached to the lowly—these are the results when the Moon or Ascendant is in the signs of Saturn. These results of the subdivisions of Mars and other planets should be understood based on the strength of the planetary combinations." 

**Furthermore, in another text:** "In the sign of Saturn, the subdivision of Mars makes one a servant, an unchaste woman, or a girl whose children die. Born in the subdivision of Saturn, a woman is an enemy to the lowly and is unfortunate. ||1||
In the subdivision of Jupiter, she is beloved by her husband, fortunate, and famous by many names. In the subdivision of Mercury, she is a foremost observer of vows, yet unchaste and very deceitful. In the subdivision of Venus, she has the nature of a lord, is barren, and has eyes fixed on conduct <note>possibly implying she is observant or critical</note>. Thus should the results of the subdivisions be told by the astrologers <term>Daivavid: one who knows the divine/fate; an astrologer</term> regarding women." ||3||

### Now, the results according to the <term>Trishansha</term><note>A 30th division of a zodiac sign used in Vedic astrology to determine specific character traits</note> in the House of the Sun

> If the Ascendant or the Moon is in the house of the Sun <note>Leo</note> and falls in the division of Mars, the woman wanders according to her own will. In the division of Mercury, she is of a very wicked mind. In the division of Jupiter, she is said to be a queen. In the division of Venus, she is devoted to unchastity. In the division of Saturn, she is very bold, behaving like a man. ||13||

**Meaning—** If at the time of a woman's birth, the Ascendant and the Moon are situated in the Sun's sign <note>Leo</note> and fall within the <term>Trishansha</term> of Mars, then that woman wanders according to her own whims. If the same Ascendant and Moon are in the division of Mercury, she is extremely wicked. If the same Ascendant and Moon are in the division of Jupiter, she becomes a queen <note>or a woman of high status</note>. If the same Ascendant and Moon are in the division of Venus, that girl is intent on the work of a prostitute. And if the same Ascendant and Moon are in the division of Saturn, that woman is bold and behaves like a man. ||13||

And so it is said in the *Horamakaranda*: "In the Moon's sign, she may be self-willed, an artist, virtuous, ill-behaved, or lose her luster. In the Sun's sign, she is talkative, acts like a man, is a wife to a traveler, or unchaste." ||1||
Another source says: "In the Sun's house: in the division of Mars, she is talkative and an adulteress living abroad. In the division of Saturn, she is skillful but of poor character and impoverished, yet considered a beloved. ||1|| In the division of Mercury, she has the appearance and character of a man, is a thief of work, and unchaste. In the division of Venus, she is dear to a bad husband, has few children, and is constantly diseased." ||2||

### Now, the results according to the division in the House of the Moon

> In the house of the Moon <note>Cancer</note>, in the division of Mars, she is self-willed; in the division of Mercury, she is skilled in crafts. In the division of Jupiter, she possesses good qualities; in the division of Venus, she is a virtuous wife; and in the division of Saturn, she is the killer of her beloved's life. ||14||

**Meaning—** For a woman whose Ascendant and Moon are situated in the house of the Moon <note>Cancer</note> and...

# Female Horoscopy
<note>original: "Stri-Jatakam" - a specialized branch of astrology focusing on women's destinies.</note>

If the Moon or Ascendant is situated in the <term>Trimsamsha</term> <note>A specific astrological division of 1 degree within a zodiac sign</note> of **Mars**, then that woman will be one who wanders according to her own whims. If that same Ascendant or Moon is situated in the Trimsamsha of **Mercury**, then that woman is clever in the works of masonry or royal architecture. If that same Ascendant or Moon is situated in the Trimsamsha of **Jupiter**, then that woman possesses excellent virtues. If that same Ascendant or Moon is situated in the Trimsamsha of **Venus**, then that woman is a devoted and faithful wife. And if that same Ascendant or Moon is situated in the Trimsamsha of **Saturn**, then that woman brings destruction to the life of her husband. || 14 || Furthermore, it is stated in another text—

> "In the house of the Moon <note>the sign of Cancer</note>, if in the portion of **Mars**, she is a self-willed woman of loose character whose children perish.
> In the portion of **Saturn**, she is bereft of a husband and gains her livelihood with great difficulty. || 1 ||
> In the portion of **Jupiter**, she has few children and is physically weak; in the portion of **Mercury**, she is a craftswoman or artist.
> In the portions of **Venus**, she is either barren, has children who die, or is of a very wicked nature. || 2 ||"

->The results of the Trimsamsha for her should be indicated from the combined longitudinal positions of the Moon and the Sun.<-
->Or, one should indicate the strength of the Trimsamsha based on the combined power of the Ascendant and the Moon. || 15 ||<-

->Thus ends the fourth chapter, named 'The Description of Results according to the Trimsamsha,' in the Female Horoscopy composed by the Royal Astrologer Pandit Shyam Lal, son of Shri Baldev Prasad, the ornament of the Gauda lineage residing in Shri Vamshavareli.<-

Meaning—The results for that woman should be told through the Trimsamsha based on the clear mathematical combination of the Moon and the Sun, or one should state the Trimsamsha results from the strength of the Ascendant and the Moon. || 15 ||

->Thus ends the fourth chapter, named 'The Description of Trimsamsha Results,' in the 'Shyam Sundari' Hindi commentary made by the Royal Astrologer Pandit Shyam Lal, son of Shri Baldev Prasad, the ornament of the Gauda lineage residing in Shri Vamshavareli.<-

<summary>This page concludes the fourth chapter of the Stri-Jatakam, detailing how a woman's character and fate are shaped by the planetary divisions (Trimsamsha) of the Moon and Ascendant. It provides specific predictions ranging from virtuous devotion to widowhood or professional skill based on planetary influences.</summary>

## Now, the Yoga for Female-to-Female Intimacy from the Saravali

> If Venus and Saturn are situated in each other's Navamsha <note>a 1/9th divisional chart used for fine detail</note> and are within each other's line of sight, or if the rising sign is a portion of Aquarius within a sign ruled by Venus, women experience an intensely increased fire of passion and find satisfaction with other women who take on a masculine form. ॥1॥

**Meaning—**In a woman's birth chart, if <term>Venus</term> <note>the planet of desire</note> and <term>Saturn</term> <note>the planet of restriction or unconventionality</note> are placed in each other's <term>Navamsha</term>—meaning Saturn is in a Navamsha of Venus and Venus is in a Navamsha of Saturn—and they look at one another, this is one combination. Alternatively, if the birth ascendant is <term>Taurus</term> or <term>Libra</term> <note>signs ruled by Venus</note> and the <term>Aquarius</term> Navamsha is rising, then that woman calms the fire of the God of Love by having another woman bind a phallic object to her thigh, transforming her into a masculine figure. ॥1॥ <margin>Varaha: <note>referring to the astrologer Varahamihira</note></margin>

> "When Saturn and Venus are in each other's portions and aspecting one another, or if the rising portion is Aquarius within a sign of Venus, a woman brings peace to the blazing poison-fire of desire through women standing in the likeness of men."

Furthermore, in the <term>Horamakaranda</term>:
> "If the son of the Sun (Saturn) and Venus have exchanged places, or if the ascendant is in a sign of Venus with an Aquarius portion, the lotus-eyed woman greatly pacifies the fire of passion through those who engage in masculine conduct." ॥2॥

In the <term>Jatakabharana</term>:
> "When Venus and Saturn are in each other's signs and aspecting each other, or when the ascendant is a sign of Venus with an Aquarius portion, the woman brings complete peace to her desire through women who have made themselves into masculine forms." ॥3॥

## Now, the Yoga for a Contemptible Husband from the Garga Jataka

> If the seventh house is empty, weak, and aspected by malefic planets, and is devoid of the sight of benefic planets, the husband will be a contemptible man. ॥12॥

**Meaning—**In a woman's birth chart, if there is no planet in the seventh house <note>the house of marriage</note> from the birth ascendant, and the seventh house is weak, and malefic planets <note>such as Mars or Saturn</note> look upon the seventh house while it lacks the gaze of auspicious planets, then that woman's husband [will be...]

It cites several classical authorities including the Saravali, Varahamihira, and Garga to corroborate these interpretations.</summary>

The husband will be lazy and lacking in effort. ### On the Yoga for an Impotent Husband

->**If Mercury and Saturn are in the seventh house, the husband will be like a eunuch.**<-
->**The woman will be barren or unfortunate, and always traveling abroad.** (3)<-

**Meaning**—In the birth chart of a woman, if <term>Mercury</term> <note>Budha</note> and <term>Saturn</term> <note>Shani</note> are situated in the seventh house, that woman's husband is like a eunuch <note>impotent or lacking virility</note>. That woman is either barren or of ill-fortune, and she constantly wanders in foreign lands. (3)
**Varaha:** "If Mercury and Saturn are in the seventh house, he is a eunuch." **Horamakaranda:** "If Mercury and Saturn are joined in the house of desire, he is a eunuch." **Jatakabharana:** "If the seventh house belongs to Mercury and Saturn, bondage <note>or a restricted life</note> will certainly occur." (1)

### On the Yoga for a Husband Who Travels

->**If the seventh house is a movable sign and its lord is in a movable division,**<-
->**The husband will be prone to traveling; if in a fixed sign, he stays at home.** (4)<-

**Meaning**—In the birth chart of a girl, if there is a <term>Movable Sign</term> <note>Chara Rashi: Aries, Cancer, Libra, or Capricorn</note> in the seventh house from the ascendant, and the lord of the <term>Seventh House</term> is also situated in a movable <term>Navamsha</term> <note>a one-ninth divisional chart used for fine-tuning predictions</note>, then that girl's husband always remains in foreign lands. If a fixed sign is situated in the seventh house and the lord of the seventh house is in a fixed Navamsha, then that woman's husband always stays at home. If the seventh house is a dual sign <note>Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, or Pisces</note> and the <term>Lord of the Seventh</term> <note>Saptamesh</note> is situated in the Navamsha of these same signs, then that woman's husband stays both in foreign lands and at home...

### Now, the Yoga for Abandonment

->If the Sun is in the setting sign <note>the 7th house</note> and is aspected by enemies, she will be abandoned.<-

In the *Horamakaranda*: "Abandoned by loved ones when the Sun is in the setting sign." **Varaha:** "Abandoned because of the Sun."

### Now, the Yoga for Widowhood while still a Virgin

Meaning—The woman in whose seventh house Mars is situated becomes a child-widow. (5). **Varaha:** "With the Sun or Mars in the setting sign, she becomes a widow in childhood." **Gunakara:** "Even in childhood, widowhood is predicted if Mars is present." In another text: "With the Son of the Earth, she truly becomes a widow in childhood"—thus there are many such statements.

### Now, the Yoga for Remaining Unmarried

->the girl, becoming a widow <note>metaphorically, or losing her betrothed</note>, reaches old age as a maiden. (6)<-

Meaning—If at the time of a girl's birth, Saturn is sitting in the seventh sign from the ascending sign and is similarly seen by enemy planets, then that girl reaches old age without being married. (6). Likewise: "And when a malefic planet..."

# Female Horoscopy

If Saturn <note>original: "mandage," referring to the slow-moving planet Saturn</note> aspects the seventh house, the young woman will become like an old woman. Furthermore, "If malefic planets aspect the divisional charts, the girl remains a virgin until she is old." It is also said: "If the seventh house is aspected by inauspicious planets, the girl reaches old age while still a maiden."

## On the Yoga for Widowhood

->If malefic planets are in the setting house <note>the 7th house of marriage</note> and in malefic signs, she becomes a widow.<-

Meaning—The woman in whose birth chart malefic planets are seated in the seventh house, or if that house falls in malefic signs, becomes a widow. As stated: "Widowhood is caused by cruel planets residing in the house of passion <note>the 7th house</note>." In the *Brihat Jataka* <note>A foundational 6th-century Sanskrit text on astronomy and astrology by Varahamihira</note>: "Widowhood occurs when fiery planets are in the setting sign." Additionally: "If weak malefic planets are in the house of the spouse and are not aspected by benefics, she is abandoned by her lord." According to some opinions:

## On the Yoga for Remarriage

->If the seventh house is occupied by both benefic and malefic planets, she will be a 'Punarbhu' <note>a woman who marries a second time</note>. ||7||<-

Meaning—The woman in whose seventh house both auspicious and inauspicious planets are seated will be married twice. ||7|| Varaha says: "With mixed planets, she becomes a remarried woman." Also: "By mixed planets residing in the house of passion, she becomes a 'Punarbhu'." In another text: "With mixed planets in the house of the beloved, she becomes a 'Punarbhu'."

## On the Yoga for Being Abandoned by the Husband

->If a weak malefic is in the setting house and is not aspected by benefic planets,<-
->the woman is separated from her husband; if it is in a debilitated or enemy sign, she becomes a 'Svarini' <note>an independent or unchaste woman</note>. ||8||<-

Meaning—The woman in whose birth chart a malefic planet lacking strength is situated in the seventh house, and no auspicious planets aspect it, is abandoned by her husband. If that same malefic planet in the seventh house is in its sign of debilitation or in the sign of an enemy, she becomes unchaste. ||8|| Gunakara says: "If a malefic is in the seventh house without strength, she is deserted by her lover unless aspected by a benefic." Varaha states: "If a cruel planet of low strength is in the setting house and is aspected by a benefic, she is cast off by her own husband." And so on.

## Now, the Combination for an Adulterous Woman in the Garga Jataka

> **If Venus and Mars are placed in each other’s Navamsha <note>A Navamsha is a one-ninth division of a zodiac sign, used in Vedic astrology to determine the hidden strength of planets</note>, the woman becomes attached to a paramour.**

Meaning—If at the time of a woman's birth, Mars is situated in the Navamsha of Venus, and Venus is situated in the Navamsha of Mars, that woman goes to another man. As stated in the *Varaha* <note>Referring to the Varahamihira's Brihat Jataka</note>: “When Mars and Venus occupy each other’s Navamsha, the woman is said to be unchaste.” In the *Hora Makaranda*: “If Venus and the son of the Earth (Mars) are in each other’s Navamsha, the woman is attached to another.” Also: “If Mars and Venus are positioned in each other’s Navamsha, she will surely have unions with other men.”

## Now, the Combination for Misconduct by the Husband’s Command

> **Similarly, if the Moon is in the seventh house, she commits misconduct by the command of her husband. ||9||**

Meaning—If at the time of a woman's birth, Venus, the Moon, and Mars are situated in the seventh house <note>The house of marriage and partnerships</note>, that woman indulges in other men with the permission of her husband. ||9|| It is also said in the *Jatakabharana*: “If Venus and Mars are in the house of desire (the 7th) along with the Moon, they say she follows the very command of her lord (husband).” Furthermore: “If the Moon and Venus are in the house of passion, it is with the lover's consent.” According to the words of *Varaha*: “Or if they (Venus and Mars) are with the Moon in the seventh house, then it is by the husband's permission.”

## Now, the Combination for Barrenness

> **When Saturn, Mars, and the Sun are in the Ascendant, and the Moon and Venus are also there,**
> **That woman becomes barren if the fifth house is aspected by malefic planets. ||10||**

Meaning—If at the time of a woman's birth, the Ascendant (Lagna) falls in a sign ruled by Saturn, Mars, or the Sun, and the Moon and Venus are seated therein, and if the fifth house <note>The house representing children and progeny</note> is aspected by malefic planets, then that woman is barren (unable to conceive). ||10||

## Now, the Combination for Diseases of the Reproductive Organs

> **When Mars is in a sign of the Sun, or if the Sun and Mars are in their own Navamshas...**

### The Yoga for a Beautiful Form

> If the descending sign is aspected by benefics, or if there is a benefic Navamsha aspected by benefics,
> The bride in the house will have beautiful hips, be dear to her husband, and be well-beloved. ||12||

**Meaning—**In a woman's birth chart, if the sign located in the seventh house is aspected by benefic planets <note>Jupiter, Venus, or Mercury</note> (this is the first combination); or if a benefic planet's Navamsha rises in the seventh house and is aspected by benefic planets, then that woman's form is excellent, she is beloved by her husband, and she is dear to everyone in her household. ||12|| As it is said: **"She has beautiful hips and is well-beloved when in a benefic planet's division."**

## Now, the Combination for Adultery with the Mother

**Meaning—**In a woman's birth chart, if the Moon joined with Mercury is situated in the <term>Lagna</term><note>The Ascendant or first house</note> in the signs of Capricorn, Aquarius, Aries, or Scorpio <note>Signs 10, 11, 1, and 8</note>, and is aspected by malefic planets, then that woman becomes an adulteress along with her mother. ||13|| Varaha <note>Referring to the sage Varahamihira</note>

### Now, the results of the Sun in its own Navamsha in the Seventh House

Varaha states: "He becomes a very gentle performer of the act of love when the Sun is in Leo in the descendant." Gunakara says: "In the Sun's portion or sign, there is gentle love." ### Now, the results of the Moon in its own Navamsha in the Seventh House

> If the Moon is in the descendant in its own sign and Navamsha, the husband is gentle and subject to the power of desire. ||14||

**Meaning—**In the birth chart of a woman, if the Moon is situated in the seventh house in its own sign and Navamsha, then that woman's husband is soft and controlled by lust. ||14|| As it is said: "In the Moon's case, he is very intoxicated with passion and gentle," and also "Subject to the power of Cupid and gentle when the Moon is present."

### Now, the results of Mars in its own Navamsha in the Seventh House

> If Mars is in the descendant in its own Navamsha and field, the husband is fond of women and poor.

**Meaning—**In the woman's seventh house, if Mars is situated in its own sign and Navamsha, that woman's husband is a lover of women and devoid of wealth. As it is said: "In the case of the Son of Earth <note>Mars</note>, he is dear to women and possessed of anger," and also "He shall be fond of women and angry." Furthermore: "The Son of Earth makes one angry and fond of women."

### Now, the results of Mercury in its own Navamsha in the Seventh House

> If Mercury is in the descendant in its own Navamsha and field, the husband shall be learned and happy. ||15||

**Meaning—**In the birth chart of a woman, if Mercury is situated in the seventh house in its own sign and Navamsha, then that girl's husband is a scholar and happy. ||15||

As it is said, "Under Mercury, the husband is learned and very skillful," and likewise, "The husband is a scholar under Mercury," and elsewhere, "Under Mercury, the husband is wise and possesses great dexterity."

->**Now, the results of Jupiter’s Sign and Division in the Seventh House**<-

Meaning—If, at the time of a woman's birth, the sign or division of Venus rises in the seventh house, that woman's husband is fortunate. (16) As it is said, "Under Venus, she is endowed with good fortune," and elsewhere, "Under Venus, he is possessed of luck," and specifically, "Under Venus, the beloved is exceedingly fortunate."

->**Now, the results of Saturn’s Sign and Division in the Seventh House**<-

->If Saturn is in the descendant in its own division or sign, the husband will be old and a fool.<-
->In this manner, one should declare the results for men based on the planets situated in the seventh sign. (17)<-

Meaning—For a woman who has the sign or division of Saturn <note>Sanskrit: Shanaishvara; the slow-moving planet associated with age and restriction.</note> rising in the seventh house, her husband is old and foolish. In this way, one should describe the effects of the planets and signs situated in the seventh house at the time of a woman's birth. (17)

->The results of the descending sign have been told, as well as the results of the rising sign.<-
->These results for the husband and wife occur surely through the strength of the planetary combinations. (18)<-

Meaning—The results of the sign situated in the seventh house have been stated; in the same way, one should understand the results of the rising sign <note>Sanskrit: Lagna; the Ascendant.</note> for a man. Thus, the results should be declared by the strength of the planetary combinations of both the woman and the man. (18)

<summary>This page details astrological predictions for a woman's husband based on whether Jupiter, Venus, or Saturn governs the seventh house of her birth chart. It concludes by noting that these planetary strengths apply reciprocally to the husband's own rising sign.</summary>

## Now, the Results of Planets Situated in the Seventh Sign

If Venus and the Moon are placed in the seventh house <note>the house of marriage and partnerships</note>, they make a woman envious yet endowed with happiness. If Mercury and the Moon are there, she possesses excellent skills in the arts and superior virtues. Furthermore, if Venus and Mercury are in the seventh, she is radiant, fortunate, and beautiful through her knowledge of clever arts. If auspicious planets <term>Benefics: planets like Jupiter, Venus, or Mercury that bring positive influence</term> are in the ascendant <note>the first house or 'body'</note>, she possesses various ornaments, virtuous qualities, fine garments, and happiness. However, if malefic planets are in the ascendant, the results are the opposite and unfavorable. ||19||

**Meaning**—The woman at whose birth Venus and the Moon are seated in the seventh house is possessed of happiness along with envy. If Mercury and the Moon are seated in the seventh position, that woman is possessed of the best arts and is highly virtuous. If Venus and Mercury are situated in the seventh position, that woman is beautiful, knowing the clever arts of a shining fortune. And the woman in whose birth-ascendant <note>Lagna</note> auspicious planets are seated, she is endowed with many types of jewelry, clothes, excellent qualities, and happiness. If sinful planets <term>Malefics: such as Mars or Saturn</term> are in the ascendant, they give the opposite, undesirable results. ||19||

> As stated in the *Horamakaranda*: "If Venus and the Moon are in the ascendant <note>Note: The commentary interprets the verse as the seventh house, though the quote mentions the Lagna/Ascendant</note>, the woman is devoted to happiness; with Mercury and the Moon, she is a knower of arts, endowed with comfort and rich in virtues; with the son of Bhrigu (Venus) and Mercury, a beautiful form is predicted. In all three of these cases, she is endowed with much wealth, happiness, and virtue, and likewise if benefics are present." Varaha <note>The famous astronomer Varahamihira</note> says: "Envious but devoted to happiness when the Moon and Venus are in the ascendant; with Mercury and the Moon, skilled in arts, happy, and rich in virtues; with Venus and Mercury, she is fortunate, radiant, and a knower of arts; in all three, she has much wealth, happiness, and virtue if benefics are present."

## Now, the Yoga for Happiness in the Paternal Home

When the Moon rises in a sign owned by a benefic planet and is joined by Venus, that bride is happy in her father's house, though she is a woman of a constantly restless nature. ||20||

**Meaning**—At the time of birth of a woman, if the Moon rises in the domain of a benefic... <note>The text cuts off here at the end of the page</note>

That woman is endowed with many pleasant qualities, and if the aforementioned planets are joined with Jupiter, then that woman is blessed with the happiness of sons and wealth. (23)

### Now, the Combination for Widowhood

->If a malefic planet is in the eighth house, she becomes a widow, especially if it is in a malefic sign.<-

**Meaning—** If at the time of a woman's birth, a malefic planet <term>Papagraha: planets like Mars or Saturn that are traditionally considered to bring hardship</term> is situated in the eighth house from the birth ascendant, that woman becomes a widow. If that same malefic planet in the eighth house is also in a zodiac sign owned by a malefic planet, she most certainly becomes a widow.

### A Combination where even Malefics give Auspicious Results

->Malefic planets situated in their exalted signs in the seventh house bestow auspicious results. (24)<-

**Meaning—** For those women who have malefic planets in the seventh house <note>the house of marriage</note> at the time of birth, but those planets are in their exalted state <note>the sign where a planet is at its maximum strength</note>, they become givers of auspicious results. (24)

### And so, the Combination for Widowhood in another text:

->Widowhood occurs when a malefic planet is situated in the eighth house.<-

**Meaning—** If a malefic planet is situated in the eighth house at the time of a girl's birth, she becomes a widow.

### Now, the Combination for the Time of Death

->Death should be predicted during the planetary period of the lord of the Navamsha<-
->in which the lord of the eighth house is situated.<-

**Meaning—** One should predict the death of that woman during the <term>Dasha: a planetary period or "ruling" time in a person's life</term> of the planet in whose <term>Navamsha: a one-ninth divisional chart used for fine-tuning predictions</term> the lord of the eighth house <note>the house of longevity and death</note> is placed.

### Now, the Combination for Death by One's Own Fault

->If benefic planets are in the second house, death occurs by one's own self. (25)<-

<page-num>64</page-num>
<header>Female Horoscopy <note>original: "Stri-jataka"; the branch of astrology concerning women's lives</note></header>

Meaning—And if a benefic planet <note>a "good" planet like Jupiter or Venus</note> is situated in the second house, then that maiden dies without the fault of widowhood <note>meaning she dies while her husband is still alive</note>. (25)

And so says Varahamihira <note>a famous 6th-century Indian astronomer and astrologer</note>: "If a malefic <note>a "cruel" planet like Mars or Saturn</note> is in the eighth house, widowhood occurs; if the lord of the eighth house is in the navamsha <note>a specific divisional chart</note> of a malefic, she becomes a widow during that planet's period. But if benefics are in the second house, she herself passes away first." Furthermore: "With a malefic in the eighth, widowhood is predicted in the year corresponding to the lord of the eighth's position; but with benefics in the second, her own death occurs first."

### On the Yoga for the Wife's Death Before the Husband

> If the waning Moon is in the eighth house, widowhood certainly occurs during its period.
> If a benefic is in the eighth house of a maiden, her own death occurs before her husband's. (26)

Meaning—If at the time of a woman's birth, the waning Moon, Sun, Mars, or Saturn are in the eighth house <note>the house of longevity and death</note>, that woman becomes a widow. This widowhood certainly occurs during the major or minor periods of the aforementioned planets. And for a maiden who has benefic planets sitting in the eighth, that woman's death occurs before her husband's. (26)

### On the Yoga for the Simultaneous Death of Husband and Wife

> When both malefic and benefic planets are joined in that house, death occurs at the same time.
> The wise should determine this after knowing the relative strength and weakness of the planets of both partners. (27)

Meaning—If at the time of a woman's birth, both malefic and benefic planets are situated in the eighth house, then that woman and her husband both attain death at the same time. A scholar should pronounce the result after knowing the strength of the planets of both the woman and the man. (27)

### Likewise, the Yoga for Simultaneous Death in the Jatakabharana <note>a 15th-century astrological treatise</note>:

> If the eighth house has mixed strength, being aspected by or joined with both benefic and malefic planets,
> All astrologers know this to be the simultaneous death of the couple.
> Or if the lords of the seventh and first houses are in one place, or if the lord of the seventh is in the first,
> Or the lord of the first is in the seventh joined with a benefic, their death is equal. (27)

### On the Combination for Many Sons

> If the house of children is occupied or aspected by benefic planets, there will be a great number of them.

**Meaning**—If, at the time of a girl's birth, benefic planets <note>planets like Jupiter, Venus, or Mercury that bring positive influence</note> are situated in or casting their gaze upon the fifth house <note>the astrological house governing progeny</note>, that woman will have many sons. Furthermore, in the *Garga Jataka* <note>an ancient astrological treatise attributed to the sage Garga</note> it is said: "**With benefic planets in the house of children, one should predict many births. At the time of giving a girl in marriage, one should consider the path described here.**"

### On the Combination for Great Suffering

> With malefic planets situated in the eighth house from the Ascendant, she is endowed with the fruit of sorrow. If they are unmixed with benefic planets, she will experience distress in every way. ||31||

**Meaning**—A woman in whose birth chart malefic planets <note>planets like Mars or Saturn that bring challenges</note> are situated in the eighth house from the Ascendant <note>the house of longevity and marital bonds</note> will always be filled with sorrow. Even if both benefic and malefic planets are situated together in that eighth house, that woman will still constantly experience hardship. ||31||

### On the Combination for Masculine Behavior

> When Mercury, the Moon, and Venus are weak, and Saturn is of middling strength, while the remaining planets are endowed with power and situated in odd-numbered signs at the Ascendant, the young woman born is always "man-like"; she behaves with masculine tendencies and becomes famous in the world for this. ||32||

**Meaning**—If, at the time of a woman's birth, Mercury, the Moon, and Venus are powerless, Saturn has moderate strength, and all the remaining planets are powerful and situated in odd-numbered signs <note>Aries, Gemini, Leo, Libra, Sagittarius, and Aquarius</note>, the girl born under such a combination possesses a masculine nature and becomes a leader among women for her character in the world. ||32||

> If Venus, the Moon, and Mercury are sparse <note>weak or poorly placed</note> and Saturn is of middling strength...

-> If Venus, the Moon, and Mercury are weak, and Saturn possesses moderate strength, while the remaining planets are powerful and situated in an odd-numbered <note>masculine</note> sign, then that woman will particularly possess the bold and daring nature of a man. <-
